On the Decidability of Verification under Release/Acquire Conference: ; ; CCS: Software and its engineering Formal software verification CCS: Theory of computation Logic and verification
Giovanna Kobus Conrado, Andreas Pavlogiannis

Abstract
. The verification of concurrent programs under weak-memory models is a burgeoning effort, owing to the increasing adoption of weak memory in concurrent software and hardware. Release/Acquire has become the standard model for high-performance concurrent programming, adopted by common mainstream languages and computer architectures. In a surprising result, Abdulla et al. (PLDI’19) proved that reachability in this model is undecidable when programs have access to atomic Read-Modify-Write (RMW) operations. Moreover, undecidability holds even for executions with just 4 contexts, and is thus immune to underapproximations based on bounded context switching. The canonical, RMW-free case was left as a challenging question, proving a non-primitive recursive lower bound as a first step, and has remained open for the past seven years. In this paper, we settle the above open question by proving that reachability is undecidable even in the RMW-free fragment of Release/Acquire, thereby characterizing the simplest set of communication primitives that gives rise to undecidability. Moreover, we prove that bounding both the number of context switches and the number of RMWs recovers decidability, thus fully characterizing the boundary of decidability along the dimensions of RMW-bounding and context-bounding.



1.
Introduction

The standard sequential view of concurrent programs
(
18
)
allows to reason about program executions in terms of concrete interleavings between different threads.
Although this view is intuitive and reflects the mental model of most practitioners, it fails to faithfully account for modern program executions, where compiler and architectural optimizations may cause threads to hold divergent views of the shared memory.
Weak memory models
(
24
;
5
;
10
;
29
;
22
)
are formal specifications of such weak executions that a program may exhibit due to such divergence, and are becoming a robust and standard means for rigorous high-performance concurrent programming.

One of the most fundamental modern concurrency models follows the Release/Acquire paradigm, which constitutes the realization of message-passing-style communication over shared memory.
In high level,

(i)

interthread communication is causally consistent, as per standard in message passing, and

(ii)

the shared memory further imposes a coherence order on each memory location, which requires that all communication via each location appears sequentially consistent.

The Release/Acquire model is found in the concurrency semantics of several modern programming languages and hardware platforms
(
10
;
22
)
.

As the behavior of a concurrent program depends on the underlying memory model, program-verification algorithms are memory-model dependent.
The most common verification question is state reachability:
“can the program reach a particular state, e.g., one that violates an assertion?”
.
Although reachability under Sequential Consistency has been long known to be
\mathsf{PSPACE}
-complete
(
16
;
27
)
, its study under weak memory is an ongoing effort in the programming languages community.
The intricacies of weak interthread interaction make the design of verification algorithms particularly challenging, while the problem itself is typically of much higher complexity (e.g., non-primitive recursive for
\mathsf{TSO}
/
\mathsf{PSO}

(
6
)
).

Verification under Release/Acquire was studied in
(
2
)
, where the problem was shown to be undecidable.
The authors found this
“[…] quite a surprising result considering the simplicity and intuitiveness [of Release/Acquire semantics]”
.
This undecidability becomes even more startling when contrasted to close variants of Release/Acquire, namely Strong/Weak/Localized Release/Acquire, which enjoy decidable reachability
(
17
;
26
)
.

Interestingly, the undecidability proof of
(
2
)
makes intricate use of the somewhat complex atomic Read-Modify-Write (RMW) operations, and does not hold for programs that only access the shared memory via traditional reads and writes.
The decidability over RMW-free programs was left as a challenging open problem, establishing a non-primitive recursive lower bound as a first step, via a reduction from lossy channel systems
(
1
;
25
)
.
This question has remained open for the past seven years, also resurfacing in other works in the meantime
(
17
;
26
)
.

Our contributions.

Our first contribution settles the above open question.

Theorem 1.1.

State reachability for RMW-free programs under Release/Acquire is undecidable.

Theorem

1.1
strengthens the undecidability of
(
2
)
to the minimal setting of programs with only read and write memory operations.
This is, perhaps, even more surprising, considering that RMW-free programs have fundamental limitations under Release/Acquire
(
12
)
, e.g., they cannot implement lock-free mutual exclusion in the style of the classical protocols by Petersen
(
20
)
and Dekker
(
14
)
.

Our proof is via a reduction from Posts’ Correspondence Problem (PCP), following the high-level approach of
(
2
)
, where a set of guesser threads guesses a solution to PCP and communicates it to a set of verifier threads.
The crux of that proof is a “no-skipping” property, where the verifier threads make heavy use of RMWs in order to not skip any part of the solution communicated by the guesser threads.
In the RMW-free case, achieving no skipping is considerably more challenging, and it has been open whether it can be enforced with plain read and write operations.

One standard approach to tractable verification of concurrent programs is by under-approximating program behavior by bounding the number of context switches between threads
(
23
)
.
Unfortunately, the proof of
(
2
)
shows that, under Release/Acquire with RMWs, undecidability holds even with just 4-context executions, and thus bounding context switches is not sufficient to make the problem decidable.
On the other hand, the no-skipping property in our reduction behind
Theorem

1.1
uses an unbounded number of context switches.
It is thus natural to ask: do bounded context switches recover decidability for RMW-free programs?
We show that this is indeed the case.

Theorem 1.2.

State reachability for RMW-free programs under Release/Acquire is decidable when restricted to executions with a bounded number of context switches.
Moreover, the problem remains decidable for general programs when the number of RMWs in an execution is also bounded.

Theorem

1.1
and
Theorem

1.2
, along with the undecidability of
(
2
)
, fully characterize the decidability landscape for reachability under Release/Acquire, with respect to context switches and RMWs: bounding the number of each is both sufficient and necessary for decidability.

2.
Preliminaries

In this section we define concurrent programs and their Release/Acquire semantics.

General notation.

Given an integer
i\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, we let
[i]=\{1,\cdots,i\}
.
For a binary relation
B
, we write
B^{?}
,
B^{+}
and
B^{*}
for the reflexive, transitive and reflexive-transitive closure of
B
, respectively, while
B^{-1}
denotes the inverse of
B
.
We often write
e_{1}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$B$}}e_{2}
to denote that
(e_{1},e_{2})\in B
, and call this a
B
-edge.
Given two binary relations
A
,
B
, we denote by
A;B
their composition.
Given a set
X
, we let
[X]
be the identity relation over
X
.

We take
\mathsf{Val}\subseteq\mathbb{N}
,
\mathsf{Loc}\subseteq\{x,y,\dots\}
,
\mathsf{Tid}\subseteq\{t_{1},t_{2},\dots\}
to be finite sets of values, shared memory locations and thread identifiers, respectively.

2.1.
Concurrent Programs

We define sequential programs as labeled transitions systems (LTS).
At a high level, a sequential program
\mathsf{Pseq}_{t}
of some thread
t\in\mathsf{Tid}
is an LTS in which each of its transitions emits a letter indicating an interaction with the shared memory via writes, reads and read-modify-writes.
A concurrent program
\mathsf{P}
is the composition of its corresponding sequential programs.

Labeled Transition Systems.

A
Labeled Transition System
(LTS) over an alphabet
\Sigma
is a tuple
\mathcal{A}=\langle\mathcal{Q},\mathcal{T},\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{in}},\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{fn}}\rangle
, where

(i)

\mathcal{Q}
is a finite set of states,

(ii)

\mathcal{T}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}\times\Sigma\times\mathcal{Q}
is a set of transitions,

(iii)

\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{in}}\in\mathcal{Q}
is the unique initial state, and

(iv)

\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{fn}}\in\mathcal{Q}
is the unique final state.

We use
\mathcal{A}.\mathcal{Q}
,
\mathcal{A}.\mathcal{T}
,
\mathcal{A}.\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{in}}
and
\mathcal{A}.\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{fn}}
to refer to the respective components of
\mathcal{A}
. We often write
p\xhookrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\alpha$}}q
to denote that
(p,\alpha,q)\in\mathcal{T}
. A state
q\in\mathcal{A}.\mathcal{Q}
is reachable in
\mathcal{A}
if
\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{in}}\xhookrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\alpha$}}^{*}q
.
A sequence
\sigma=\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\dots,\alpha_{n}
such that

\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{in}}\xhookrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\alpha_{1}$}}p_{1}\xhookrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\alpha_{2}$}}\dots\xhookrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\alpha_{n}$}}p_{n}
, where
p_{i}\in\mathcal{Q}
is a
word
of
\mathcal{A}
.
A word
\sigma=\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\dots,\alpha_{n}

reaches
some state
q\in\mathcal{A}.\mathcal{Q}
if

\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{in}}\xhookrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\alpha_{1}$}}p_{1}\xhookrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\alpha_{2}$}}\dots p_{n-1}\xhookrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\alpha_{n}$}}q
, where
p_{i}\in\mathcal{Q}
.

Event labels.

An
event label
is either a
read label

\mathtt{r}(t,x,v_{r})
, a
write label

\mathtt{w}(t,x,v_{w})
, or a
read-modify-write label

\mathtt{rmw}(t,x,v_{r},v_{w})
, where
t\in\mathsf{Tid}
,
x\in\mathsf{Loc}
, and
v_{r},v_{w}\in\mathsf{Val}
.
For example,
\mathtt{r}(t,x,v_{r})
denotes the action of thread
t
reading the value
v_{r}
from the shared memory location
x
.
We define
\mathsf{Lab}
to be the set of all event labels and
\mathsf{Lab}^{t}
to be the set of all labels with thread id
t
.

Sequential programs.

A
sequential program

\mathsf{Pseq}_{t}
of thread
t
is represented as an LTS over the alphabet
\mathsf{Lab}^{t}
.
A transition
p\xhookrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\alpha$}}q
of
\mathsf{Pseq}_{t}
captures the fact that
\mathsf{Pseq}_{t}
executes an instruction that writes to/reads from the shared memory, where
\alpha
labels the respective operation.
In doing so,
\mathsf{Pseq}_{t}
transitions from its state
p
to state
q
.

Concurrent programs.

A
concurrent program
(or simply a
program
)
\mathsf{P}=\langle\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{1}},\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{2}},\dots,\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{|\mathsf{Tid}|}}\rangle
is the composition of sequential programs
\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{i}}
for
t_{i}\in\mathsf{Tid}
.
The state of a concurrent program is a sequence
\langle p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{{|\mathsf{Tid}|}}\rangle
, where
p_{i}\in\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{i}}.\mathcal{Q}
.
The initial and final states of
\mathsf{P}
are
\langle\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{1}}.\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{in}},\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{2}}.\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{in}},\dots,\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{{|\mathsf{Tid}|}}}.\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{in}}\rangle
and
\langle\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{1}}.\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{fn}},\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{2}}.\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{fn}},\dots,\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{{|\mathsf{Tid}|}}}.\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{fn}}\rangle
, respectively.

2.2.
Release/Acquire Semantics

We now present the Release/Acquire memory model.

Events.

Events represent instructions of a program that interact with shared memory, and they are simply event labels (as emitted by
\mathsf{P}
) attached to some arbitrary unique identifier.
Formally, an
event

e
is a pair
\langle\mathsf{id},\mathsf{lab}\rangle
, where
\mathsf{id}\in\mathbb{N}
is an identifier and
\mathsf{lab}\in\mathsf{Lab}
is a label.
We write
\mathsf{lab}(e)
,
\mathsf{tid}(e)
,
\mathsf{loc}(e)
and
\mathsf{op}(e)
to refer to the label, thread id, location and operation, respectively, of
e
.
If
\mathsf{op}(e)\in\{\mathtt{w},\mathtt{rmw}\}
, then
e
is a
writing
event, while if
\mathsf{op}(e)\in\{\mathtt{r},\mathtt{rmw}\}
, then
e
is a
reading
event.
We write
\mathsf{val}_{\mathtt{r}}(e)
and
\mathsf{val}_{\mathtt{w}}(e)
to refer to the value read by a reading event
e
and the value written by writing event
e
, respectively.
We often identify an event only by its label, or even a partial label, when some components of the label are irrelevant or clear from the context.
For example, we may refer to an event
\mathtt{r}(t,x,v_{r})
,
\mathtt{r}(t,x)
or
\mathtt{r}(x)
.
We let
\mathsf{E}
be the domain of events;
\mathsf{R}
,
\mathsf{W}
and
\mathsf{RMW}
are its subsets containing only read, write and RMW events, respectively.

Given a set of events
X
, we let
{{X}_{x}}
denote the set of events of
X
on location
x
, i.e,
{{X}_{x}}=\{e\in X\colon\mathsf{loc}(e)=x\}
.
Similarly,
X^{t}
is the set of events of
X
on thread
t
, i.e,
X^{t}=\{e\in X\colon\mathsf{tid}(e)=t\}
.
We extend this notation to binary relations
B
over
\mathsf{E}
, e.g.,
{{B}_{x}}=\{(e_{1},e_{2})\in B\colon\mathsf{loc}(e_{1})=\mathsf{loc}(e_{2})=x\}
.

Execution graphs.

As per standard in Release/Acquire and related memory models, we
represent
program executions as
execution graphs

G=\langle E,\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}\rangle
.
We often write
G.E
,
G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
,
G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
and
G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
for the respective components of
G
.

G.E
denotes the set of events that participate in the execution.
The
program order

G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}\subseteq(\bigcup_{t\in\mathsf{Tid}}{\mathsf{E}^{t}}\times{\mathsf{E}^{t}})
captures the total order in which events of the same thread executed.
The
reads-from relation

G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}\subseteq(\bigcup_{x\in\mathsf{Loc}}({{\mathsf{W}}_{x}}\cup{{\mathsf{RMW}}_{x}})\times({{\mathsf{R}}_{x}}\cup{{\mathsf{RMW}}_{x}}))
connects every reading event to a writing event that the former obtains its value from.
Hence, we require that

(i)

the values of the connected events match, i.e.,
(e_{w},e_{r})\in\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}\implies(\mathsf{val}_{\mathtt{w}}(e_{w})=\mathsf{val}_{\mathtt{r}}(e_{r}))
, and

(ii)

a reading event has exactly one incoming edge, i.e.,
(e_{w_{1}},e_{r}),(e_{w_{2}},e_{r})\in\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}\implies(e_{w_{1}}=e_{w_{2}})
and
\forall(e_{r}\in\mathsf{R}\cup\mathsf{RMW}),\exists e_{w}\in E\colon(e_{w},e_{r})\in\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
.
Finally, the
modification order

G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}\subseteq\bigcup_{x\in\mathsf{Loc}}(({{\mathsf{W}}_{x}}\cup{{\mathsf{RMW}}_{x}})\times({{\mathsf{W}}_{x}}\cup{{\mathsf{RMW}}_{x}}))
represents a total order on the write/RMW events in each location.

e
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}

(a)

irr-hb

\mathtt{w}
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}

(b)

write-coherence

\mathtt{w}
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}
\mathtt{r}
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}

(c)

read-coherence

\mathtt{w}
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}
\mathtt{rmw}
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}

(d)

atomicity

Figure 1
.

The four axioms of Release/Acquire, phrased as forbidden patterns.

Release/Acquire semantics.

We follow the standard (see, e.g.,
(
17
)
) axiomatic definition of Release/Acquire over execution graphs
G
, phrased as axioms over the relations of
G
, as well as the derived
happens-before
relation
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}\triangleq(\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}\cup\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf})^{+}
(often interpreted as “causality”).
In particular,
G
is
Release/Acquire-consistent
if it satisfies the following irreflexivity properties (see also
Fig.

1
):

•

states that
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
is irreflexive, excluding the presence of causality cycles,

•

states that
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo};\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
is irreflexive, i.e.,
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
follows causality,

•

states that
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo};\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb};\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}^{-1}
is irreflexive, i.e., a reading event obtains its value from the
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
-maximal writing event among those that are causally before the former, and

•

states that
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo};\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo};\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}^{-1}
is irreflexive, i.e., an RMW obtains its value from the
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
-maximal writing event among those that are
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
-ordered before the former, capturing the fact that the reading and writing part of the RMW occur atomically.

Release/Acquire program semantics.

Given an execution graph
G=\langle E,\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}\rangle
and a thread
t_{i}\in\mathsf{Tid}
, there is a unique topological ordering
e_{1},e_{2},\dots,e_{n}
of
G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}^{t_{i}}
.
For such a case, we let
\mathsf{Word}(G,t_{i})=\mathsf{lab}(e_{1}),\mathsf{lab}(e_{2}),\dots,\mathsf{lab}(e_{n})
.
Given a concurrent program
\mathsf{P}=\langle\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{1}},\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{2}},\cdots,\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{{|\mathsf{Tid}|}}}\rangle
, the
Release/Acquire semantics
of
\mathsf{P}
, denoted
\llbracket{\mathsf{P}}\rrbracket
, is the set of execution graphs
G
such that

(i)

G
is Release/Acquire-consistent, and

(ii)

for all
t_{i}\in\mathsf{Tid}
, we have that
\mathsf{Word}(G,t_{i})
is a word of
\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{i}}
.

Release/Acquire reachability.

Given a concurrent program
\mathsf{P}=\langle\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{1}},\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{2}},\cdots,\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{{|\mathsf{Tid}|}}}\rangle
, a state
\langle p_{1},p_{2},\dots,p_{{|\mathsf{Tid}|}}\rangle
of
\mathsf{P}
, and an execution graph
G\in\llbracket{\mathsf{P}}\rrbracket
, we say that
G

reaches

\langle p_{1},p_{2},\dots,p_{{|\mathsf{Tid}|}}\rangle
if
\mathsf{Word}(G,t_{i})
reaches
p_{i}
for all
i\in[{|\mathsf{Tid}|}]
.
The reachability problem is to decide, given a concurrent program
\mathsf{P}
, whether there exists an execution graph
G\in\llbracket{\mathsf{P}}\rrbracket
that reaches the final state of
\mathsf{P}
, i.e.,
\langle\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{1}}.\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{fn}},\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{2}}.\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{fn}},\dots,\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{{|\mathsf{Tid}|}}}.\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{fn}}\rangle
1
1

1

The problem of requiring a
single
thread to reach the final state is equally expressive, in the sense that the two problems trivially reduce to each other.
.

3.
Undecidability of RMW-free Reachability

In this section we prove
Theorem

1.1
.
We note that the proof is somewhat combinatorially involved.
To benefit exposition, we begin with a proof overview (
Section

3.1
), followed by the formal reduction (
Section

3.2
), a proof of its soundness (
Section

3.3
) and its completeness (
Section

3.4
).

Event indexing.

For notational convenience, when we talk about an execution
G
of a concurrent program, we write
\mathtt{w}_{i}(t,x)
and
\mathtt{r}_{i}(t,x)
to refer to the
i
-th read and write, respectively, event of thread
t
on location
x
in
G
.
We may also refer to
i
as the
index
of
\mathtt{w}_{i}(t,x)
or
\mathtt{r}_{i}(t,x)
, and refer to an event of index
i
as
e_{i}
.
In our figures, these events are represented as
\mathtt{w}_{i}(x,v)
and
\mathtt{r}_{i}(x,v)
, respectively, where
v
is the value written or read; we omit the thread information since it is visually clear from context.

Post’s correspondence problem.

Post’s correspondence problem (PCP)
(
21
)
takes as input two tuples
\langle\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{n}\rangle
and
\langle\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{n}\rangle
of non-empty words over some alphabet
\Gamma
, i.e.,
\alpha_{i},\beta_{i}\in\Gamma^{*}
.
A solution to PCP is a non-empty sequence of indexes
j_{1},\cdots,j_{k}\in\{1,\cdots,n\}^{*}
such that
\alpha_{j_{1}}\cdots\alpha_{j_{k}}=\beta_{j_{1}}\cdots\beta_{j_{k}}
, where
\cdot
denotes word concatenation.
PCP is well-known to be undecidable.

3.1.
Proof Overview

The general construction.

The undecidability of reachability with RMWs was proven in
(
2
)
via a reduction from PCP.
Given an instance
\mathcal{I}
of PCP, the proof constructs a concurrent program
\mathsf{P}
such that there exists an execution in
\llbracket{\mathsf{P}}\rrbracket
in which every thread reaches a particular
\mathsf{term}
state iff
\mathcal{I}
has a solution.
We follow a similar approach here, though not having RMWs in our disposal appears to require a considerably more intricate construction.

Our program
\mathsf{P}
consists of 12 threads and 20 locations in total.
At a high level, the main threads,
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
,
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
,
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}
,
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}
,
{t_{x}}
, and
{t_{y}}
interact through the main locations,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}}
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}}
as follows (see
Fig.

2
for an illustration):

•

Threads
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
and
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
independently guess a solution to PCP, one index at a time, each by setting non-deterministically its own (i.e., thread-local) location
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}aux}
.
To ensure that they guess the same solution,
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
communicates its sequence of indices
j_{1},\cdots,j_{k}\in\{1,\dots,n\}^{*}
, one by one, to
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
via the location
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}}
, and the latter verifies that it matches its own sequence.
Moreover,
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
writes to
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}}
, one by one, the sequence
j_{1},\cdots,j_{k}
,
while
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
writes to
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}}
, letter by letter, the corresponding word
\alpha_{j_{1}}\cdots\alpha_{j_{k}}
.

•

Threads
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}
and
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}
work similarly, independently guessing the same solution to PCP, through thread-local locations
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}aux}
.
The location
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}}
is used by
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}
to communicate its sequence of indices
j^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,j^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}\in\{1,\dots,n\}^{*}
to
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}
.
Moreover, thread
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}
writes to
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
, one by one,
j^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,j^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}
, and thread
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}
writes to
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
, letter by letter, the word
\beta_{j^{\prime}_{1}}\cdots\beta_{j^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}
.

•

Thread
{t_{x}}
guesses, letter by letter, a sequence
\mathsf{lt}_{1},\cdots,\mathsf{lt}_{\ell}\in\Gamma^{*}
.
It executes a while loop that, in its
i
-th iteration, reads the
i
-th value written to
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}}
(by
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
) and the
i
-th value written to
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
(by
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}
),
and verifies that each value equals
\mathsf{lt}_{i}
.
It, thus, verifies that
\alpha_{j_{1}}\cdots\alpha_{j_{k}}=\beta_{j^{\prime}_{1}}\cdots\beta_{j^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}
.

•

Thread
{t_{y}}
guesses, index by index, a sequence
\mathsf{id}_{1},\cdots,\mathsf{id}_{m}\in\{1,\dots,n\}^{*}
.
It executes a while loop that, in its
i
-th iteration, reads the
i
-th value written to
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}}
(by
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
) and the
i
-th value written to
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
(by
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}
),
and verifies that each value equals
\mathsf{id}_{i}
.
It, thus, verifies that
j_{1},\cdots,j_{k}=j^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,j^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}
.

Figure 2
.

The high level behavior of
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
,
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
,
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}
,
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}
,
{t_{x}}
, and
{t_{y}}
on a PCP instance with the tuples
\langle ba,aa\rangle
and
\langle aba,a\rangle
as input.
The solution illustrated is the sequence
2,1
, which generates the string
aaba
.
Words guessed by
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
and
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
are shown on the left; those guessed by
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}
and
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}
, on the right.
The highlighted pairs in
{t_{x}}
and
{t_{y}}
mark operations that must read the same value.

Each of the threads ends with an instruction
\mathsf{term}
.
All threads can simultaneously reach
\mathsf{term}
if and only if the sequence
j_{1},\cdots,j_{k}
(guessed by threads
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
and
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
) and the sequence
j^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,j^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}
(guessed by threads
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}
and
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}
) are such that
\alpha_{j_{1}}\cdots\alpha_{j_{k}}=\beta_{j^{\prime}_{1}}\cdots\beta_{j^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}
(as verified by
{t_{x}}
) and
j_{1},\cdots,j_{k}=j^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,j^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}
(as verified by
{t_{y}}
).
This means that
\mathsf{P}
has an execution in which every thread reaches
\mathsf{term}
if and only if the corresponding PCP instance
\mathcal{I}
has a solution, as desired.

We refer to threads
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
,
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
,
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}
and
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}
as
guesser threads
, and to threads
{t_{x}}
and
{t_{y}}
as
verifier threads
.

The challenge.

Establishing the interaction pattern described above is tricky,
because it requires that the verifier threads do not skip any writes performed by the guesser threads.
For example, after
\mathtt{r}_{1}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
has read from
\mathtt{w}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
, there is no mechanism (yet) to disallow the next read
\mathtt{r}_{2}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
from skipping the next write
\mathtt{w}_{2}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
and instead read from
\mathtt{w}_{3}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
.
Moreover, it is essential that this “no-skipping” property is enforced without using
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edges from verifier threads back to the guesser threads.
Intuitively, this is because the thread sets
\{{t_{\alpha}^{x}},{t_{\alpha}^{y}}\}
,
\{{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}},{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}\}
,

\{{t_{x}}\}
, and
\{{t_{y}}\}
, must execute highly asynchronously to each other.
The presence of such
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
edges, together with the existing
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
edges from guesser threads to verifier threads on locations
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}}
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
, could make
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
cyclic, thereby violating
and producing an inconsistent execution.

Instead, we enforce no-skipping by utilizing
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
-edges from the verifier threads back to the guesser threads, while retaining that
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo};\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
is irreflexive, as per
2
2

2

It may be worth noting that
(\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}\cup\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo})^{+}
will not be irreflexive in general, but the irreflexivitity of this relation is only a requirement of write coherence of stronger models, such as Strong Release/Acquire.
.
Our solution introduces four new guesser threads (
{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}
,
{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}}
,
{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{x}}
,
{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{y}}
) and two new verifier threads (
t^{\prime}_{x}
, and
{t^{\prime}_{y}}
).
Each primed thread is a variant of one of the aforementioned main threads (
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
,
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
,
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}
,
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}
,
{t_{x}}
, and
{t_{y}}
), and each primed thread executes synchronously with its non-primed variant to enforce no-skipping.
For example,
{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}
executes synchronously with
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
, and
t^{\prime}_{x}
executes synchronously with
{t_{x}}
, and the interaction between these four threads ensures that
{t_{x}}
does not skip any of the writes of
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
on
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}}
.

In the remainder of this section we illustrate the full set of locations, and explain how various no-skipping variants are achieved.

3
illustrates the full communication topology of our construction.

Guesser threads.

We introduce auxiliary locations
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{x}}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{y}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x,y}}
; these, as well as
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}}
, establish synchronization between each non-primed guesser thread and its primed variant (see top and bottom rows of
Fig.

3
).
Overall, these locations enforce that each of the thread sets
\{{t_{\alpha}^{x}},{t_{\alpha}^{y}},{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}}\}
and
\{{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}},{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}},{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{x}},{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{y}}\}
execute synchronously, meaning that the threads of each set, together, produce a sequentially-consistent execution if we ignore interactions with other threads.

All events
e_{i}
on
z
-locations (
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox ) write/read a single value

.
Events
e_{i}
on
\ell
-locations (
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}}
) write/read

, where

and

of the PCP instance

.
This distinction exists because events on
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}}
(resp.,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}}
) have a double role: besides synchronizing
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
and
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
(resp.,
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}
and
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}
), they are also used for communicating the guessed PCP solution of
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
to
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
(resp.,
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}
to
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}
), so that
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
(resp.,
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}
) can verify that it matches its own guess.

Figure 4
.

An illustration of the synchronization between threads in
\{{t_{\alpha}^{x}},{t_{\alpha}^{y}},{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}}\}
.
Here
|\alpha_{j_{1}}|=2
,
|\alpha_{j_{2}}|=1
and
|\alpha_{j_{3}}|=2
, and
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
performs two writes events to
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}}
corresponding to the characters of its first guessed word (
\mathtt{w}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
and
\mathtt{w}_{2}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
), then one write event for the second (
\mathtt{w}_{3}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
), and two write events for the third (
\mathtt{w}_{4}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
and
\mathtt{w}_{5}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
).
We denote by
*
an unspecified value in an event, which corresponds to a value specific to the guessed solution of the PCP instance.

Synchronization among
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
,
{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}
,
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
, and
{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}}
.

We now illustrate how
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
,
{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}
,
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
, and
{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}}
use the aforementioned locations to interact, following the example in
Fig.

4
.
The values shown for each event in the figure are chosen deterministically by the respective threads; our analysis establishes why the
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edges must always follow this particular pattern.
In turn, this pattern is combined later with other patterns to enforce no-skipping between the guesser and verifier threads, which is our goal in the first place.

First, observe that
\mathtt{w}_{1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
(edge
1
), because
\mathtt{r}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
(event
A
) must read value
1
.
Any later write
\mathtt{w}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
that writes this value is such that
i\geq 5
(for example, event
B
).
However, any such
\mathtt{w}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
is preceded by
\mathtt{r}_{4}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})
(event
C
), which reads value
0
.
In turn, this value is only written by writes
\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})
that appear after
\mathtt{r}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
(for example, event
D
).
Hence, if
\mathtt{r}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
was to read from any write
\mathtt{w}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
with
i\geq 2
, this would cause an
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-cycle

\mathtt{w}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{4}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}}),

violating
.
Symmetric reasoning on
\mathtt{r}_{1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})
establishes that
\mathtt{w}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})
(edge
2
).
The argument repeats for the next two read events
\mathtt{r}_{2}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
and
\mathtt{r}_{2}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})
,
inductively establishing the no-skipping invariant

\forall i\quad\mathtt{w}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})

The same pattern holds for the interaction between
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
and
{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}}
over locations
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{y}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}}}
, establishing the no-skipping invariant

\forall i\quad\mathtt{w}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{y}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{y}})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}}})

Finally, a similar pattern holds for the interaction between
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
and
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
over locations
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}}
, establishing the no-skipping invariant

\forall i\quad\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}})

Synchronization among
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}
,
{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{x}}
,
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}
, and
{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{y}}
.

Threads
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}
,
{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{x}}
,
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}
, and
{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{y}}
are completely symmetric to
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
,
{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}
,
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
, and
{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}}
.
They use locations
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{x}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{y}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x,y}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}}
to form the same pattern as threads
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
,
{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}
,
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
, and
{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}}
, and locations
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{y}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}}
, respectively.
These establish the following no-skipping invariants, which are analogous to
Sections

3.1
,
3.1
and
3.1
.

to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}})

Verifier threads.

Recall that the verifier thread
{t_{x}}
reads the values of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
written by the guesser threads
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
and
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}
, and
{t_{y}}
reads
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
written by
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
and
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}
(see
Fig.

3
).
As with the guesser threads, each of the verifier threads
{t_{x}}
and
{t_{y}}
has a primed variant,
t^{\prime}_{x}
and
{t^{\prime}_{y}}
.
We also employ four additional auxiliary locations,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
.

Events
e_{i}
on
x
-locations (
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
) write/read either
\langle t,v\rangle
or
\langle\overline{t},v\rangle
,
and events
e_{i}
on
y
-locations (
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
) write/read either
\langle t,v\rangle
or
\langle\overline{t},v\rangle
.
In both cases, if
e_{i}
is a write event, the first field
t
records
\mathsf{tid}(e_{i})
(the distinction between
t
and
\overline{t}
will become clear later);
if
e_{i}
is a read event, it records the thread that
e_{i}
intends to read from.
This implies that each read event
e_{i}
reads from its intended thread, even though multiple threads write to
\mathsf{loc}(e_{i})
.
The second field
v
depends on the value of its first field
t
.
For
x
-locations,
v
is a letter
\gamma\in\Gamma
if
t\in\{{t_{\alpha}^{x}},{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}\}
, 0 if
t\in\{{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{x}}\}
, and
(i\bmod 4)
if
t\in\{{t_{x}},t^{\prime}_{x}\}
;
for
y
-locations,
v
is an index
j\in[n]
if

, and
