\displaystyle\langle\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{y}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}),\mathtt{r}_{j}({t_{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\rangle,\quad\langle\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{y}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}),\mathtt{r}_{j}({t_{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}})\rangle

In particular, all decreasing edges
\mathtt{r}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
between non-bridge read events are such that
\mathtt{r}_{i}
reads from a guesser thread.

Alternating and minimal
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-paths.

We call a
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path
alternating
if it has the form

a\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}^{?}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i_{1}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i_{1}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i_{2}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i_{2}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\cdots\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i_{\ell}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i_{\ell}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}^{?}$}}b

i.e., it is an alternating sequence of
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
and
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
edges.
We write
\mathcal{P}\colon a\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}b
to denote such an alternating
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path.
Note that if
a\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}b
, then there exists an alternating
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path of the form of
Section

3.4
from
a
to
b
.
We call
\mathcal{P}
a cycle if
a=b
and
\mathcal{P}
contains at least one edge.
We say that
\mathcal{P}
is
contained
in a thread set
\{t_{1},\dots,t_{m}\}
if all of its events
e
are such that
\mathsf{tid}(e)\in\{t_{1},\dots,t_{m}\}
.
We call
\mathcal{P}

minimal
if every
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge of
\mathcal{P}
is on a distinct thread.
Finally, we call
\mathcal{P}\colon e_{i}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}e_{j}
non-decreasing (resp., increasing) if
i\leq j
(resp.,
i<j
).
The next lemma establishes
for
G
.

Lemma 3.6.

The
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
relation is irreflexive.

Proof.

Assume towards contradiction that there is an alternating
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-cycle
\mathcal{P}\colon a\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}a
.
Since no thread reads from itself,
\mathcal{P}
cannot be contained on any single thread.
Moreover, wlog, we may assume that
\mathcal{P}
is minimal.
Then, due to the communication topology (
Fig.

3
),
\mathcal{P}
must be fully contained in one of the following pairs of threads

\{{t_{\alpha}^{x}},{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}\},\quad\{{t_{\alpha}^{x}},{t_{\alpha}^{y}}\},\quad\{{t_{\alpha}^{y}},{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}}\},\quad\{{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}},{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{x}}\},\quad\{{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}},{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}\},\quad\{{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}},{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{y}}\},\quad\{{t_{x}},t^{\prime}_{x}\},\quad\{{t_{y}},{t^{\prime}_{y}}\}\ ,

since there is no communication (i.e., no
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edges) between any other pair of threads.
Since a cycle spanning multiple threads contains a write event, and the presence of a cycle implies the presence of all its cyclic shifts, we can also assume wlog that
\mathcal{P}
starts and ends on a write event, and thus has the following form

\mathcal{P}\colon\mathtt{w}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{j}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i}\ .

Note that every event of
\mathcal{P}
accesses a location that is accessed by both threads that
\mathcal{P}
traverses.
We consider two cases, depending on the pairs of threads that
\mathcal{P}
is contained in.

Pairs
\{{t_{\alpha}^{x}},{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}\}
,
\{{t_{\alpha}^{y}},{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}}\}
,
\{{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}},{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{x}}\}
,
\{{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}},{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{y}}\}
,
\{{t_{x}},t^{\prime}_{x}\}
and
\{{t_{y}},{t^{\prime}_{y}}\}
.

None of these thread pairs share bridge locations, so all events in
\mathcal{P}
are non-bridge events.
By
Lemma

3.4
, read-to-write
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edges are increasing, and by construction,
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edges are non-decreasing.
Therefore
i<j<i
, a contradiction.

Pairs
\{{t_{\alpha}^{x}},{t_{\alpha}^{y}}\}
and
\{{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}},{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}\}
.

We first consider the thread pair
\{{t_{\alpha}^{x}},{t_{\alpha}^{y}}\}
.
The shared locations of this pair are
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}}
, hence every event in
\mathcal{P}
must access one of these two locations.
The only read-to-write
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edges connecting events in these locations are of the form
\mathtt{r}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}})
and
\mathtt{r}({t_{\alpha}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}({t_{\alpha}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}})
.
Note that
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
alternates writes to
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}}
and reads from
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}}
, starting with a write to
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}}
.
Therefore,
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edges of the form
\mathtt{r}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}})
are increasing (note, for instance,
\mathtt{r}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{2}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}})
in
Fig.

4
).
Similarly,
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
alternates writes to
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}}
and reads from
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}}
, and additionally skips the first read to
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}}
.
Therefore,
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edges of the form
\mathtt{r}({t_{\alpha}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}({t_{\alpha}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}})
are also increasing (note, for instance,
\mathtt{r}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{3}({t_{\alpha}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}})
in
Fig.

4
).
This yields
i<j<i
, a contradiction.

The same argument applies to the pair
\{{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}},{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}\}
, which follows an analogous pattern.
In this case, the respective locations are
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x,y}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}}
, and all edges of the form
\mathtt{r}({t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}({t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x,y}})
and
\mathtt{r}({t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x,y}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}({t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}})
are increasing.
∎

Besides establishing
,
Lemma

3.6
allows us to consider only minimal
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-paths between events of
G
, as stated in the following lemma.

Lemma 3.7.

For any two events
a
,
b
of
G
, if
a\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}b
then there exists a minimal
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path
\mathcal{P}\colon a\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}b
.

This result aids us in establishing further properties of
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
.
To further simplify following lemmas by exploiting the symmetry of our construction, we group threads in two sets.

Thread sets
\mathsf{Tid}_{x}
and
\mathsf{Tid}_{y}
.

In our construction, threads split naturally into two groups according to the locations they access:
\mathsf{Tid}_{x}=\{{t_{\alpha}^{x}},{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}},{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{x}},{t_{x}},t^{\prime}_{x}\}
for threads reading and writing
x
-locations, and
\mathsf{Tid}_{y}=\{{t_{\alpha}^{y}},{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}},{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}},{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{y}},{t_{y}},{t^{\prime}_{y}}\}
for
y
-locations.
This separation is key to
Lemma

3.8
,
which states that, wlog,
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-paths between non-bridge events in the same thread set are bridge-free.

Lemma 3.8.

Let
a,b
be non-bridge events such that
\mathsf{tid}(a),\mathsf{tid}(b)\in\mathsf{Tid}_{x}
or
\mathsf{tid}(a),\mathsf{tid}(b)\in\mathsf{Tid}_{y}
.
If
a\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}b
, any minimal
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path
\mathcal{P}\colon a\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}b
does not contain bridge events.

The following three lemmas state some additional monotonicity properties of
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
that will be useful later.

Lemma

3.9
establishes monotonicity properties of minimal
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-paths that avoid bridge events (for instance, the ones just established in
Lemma

3.8
),

Lemma

3.10
states that
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-edges between same-location reads on verifier threads are increasing, and
Lemma

3.11
states that certain
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-edges from writes on a primed thread to its non-primed variant increase the index by at least two.

Lemma 3.9.

Consider any minimal
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path
\mathcal{P}\colon\mathtt{w}_{i}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
that does not contain a bridge event.
Then

(i)

i\leq j
, and

(ii)

if
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})\neq\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{j})
, then
i<j
.

Lemma 3.10.

Every
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-edge
\mathtt{r}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
such that
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{r}_{i})=\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{r}_{j})
and
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{r}_{i}),\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{r}_{j})\in\{{t_{x}},t^{\prime}_{x},{t_{y}},{t^{\prime}_{y}}\}
, is such that
i<j
.

Lemma 3.11.

Every
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-edge
\mathtt{w}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
where
\langle\mathtt{w}_{i},\mathtt{w}_{j}\rangle
is one of the following pairs

\displaystyle\langle\mathtt{w}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}}),\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\rangle,\quad\langle\mathtt{w}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}}),\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{\alpha}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}})\rangle

\displaystyle\langle\mathtt{w}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}),\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}})\rangle,\quad\langle\mathtt{w}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}),\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}})\rangle

is such that
i<j-1
.

We now arrive at the two general monotonicity properties of
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
between same-location events:
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
is increasing between writes (
Lemma

3.12
), and non-decreasing from a write to a read (
Lemma

3.13
).

Lemma 3.12.

For any location
u
, we have that
[\mathsf{W}];\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}_{u};[\mathsf{W}]
is increasing.

The proof relies on the fact that for any two write events
\mathtt{w}_{i}
,
\mathtt{w}_{j}
with
\mathtt{w}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}_{u}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
, either
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})=\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{j})
or
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})\neq\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{j})
.
If
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})=\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{j})
,
Lemma

3.6
implies that
\mathtt{w}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
and thus
i<j
.
Otherwise, a case analysis on
u
shows that either
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i}),\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{j})\in\mathsf{Tid}_{x}
or
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i}),\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{j})\in\mathsf{Tid}_{y}
.
Then,
Lemma

3.7
states that there exists a minimal
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path
\mathcal{P}\colon\mathtt{w}_{i}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
,
Lemma

3.8
implies that
\mathcal{P}
does not contain bridge events, and, finally,
Lemma

3.9
implies that
\mathcal{P}
is increasing.

Lemma 3.13.

For any location
u
, we have that
[\mathsf{W}];\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}_{u};[\mathsf{R}];
is non-decreasing.

The proof proceeds by a case analysis on the shape of a minimal
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path
\mathcal{P}\colon\mathtt{w}_{i}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
; most cases follow directly from
Lemma

3.12
and the structure of each thread.
The most intricate case is when
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})
is a guesser thread,
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{r}_{j})
is a verifier thread, and
\mathcal{P}
ends in a
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge.
One example of this case, present in
Fig.

5
, is
\mathtt{w}_{1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{2}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
and the minimal
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path

\mathtt{w}_{1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{3}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{3}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{r}_{2}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}}).

The difficulty stems from the fact that, as shown in the example, the last
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge may be decreasing.
Nevertheless, notice that

(i)

this last
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge of
\mathcal{P}
decreases by
1
,

(ii)

it is preceded by the subpath
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\colon\mathtt{w}_{1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{3}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
which is increasing, and

(iii)

\mathcal{P}
does not contain any other decreasing edges,

thereby
\mathcal{P}
is non-decreasing overall.
A careful analysis shows that this pattern is not accidental.
For
Item

(i)
, the last decreasing
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge is always of the form of
Eq.

19
, and thus can only decrease by
1
, as guaranteed by
Lemma

3.5
.
For
Item

(ii)
, we prove that
\mathcal{P}
must have a subpath where
Item

(ii)
of
Lemma

3.9
applies, and thus is increasing (like
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
in our example above).
Finally,
Item

(iii)
follows from the facts that
\mathcal{P}
is bridge-free due to
Lemma

3.8
, read-to-write
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edges between non-bridge events are increasing due to
Lemma

3.4
, and
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edges are non-decreasing.

Proof of
Lemma

3.3
.

We are now ready to argue about the consistency of
G
.

Lemma

3.6
already establishes
, so it remains to argue about
and
.

.

Consider any
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
-edge
\mathtt{w}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
, and by construction, we have
i\leq j
.
By
Lemma

3.12
, we have that
\mathtt{w}_{j}\not\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize\hskip 5.0pt$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i}
.
Thus,
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo};\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
is irreflexive, as desired.

.

Consider three events
\mathtt{w}_{i}
,
\mathtt{r}_{i}
, and
\mathtt{w}_{j}
where
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{i})=\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{r}_{i})=\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{j})
.
We argue that if
\mathtt{w}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}
and
\mathtt{w}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
, then
\mathtt{w}_{j}\not\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize\hskip 5.0pt$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}
;
therefore, no triple of events can constitute a
violation.
By construction,
\mathtt{w}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
implies
i\leq j
.
If
i<j
, then
\mathtt{w}_{j}\not\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize\hskip 5.0pt$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}
follows from
Lemma

3.13
.
We are thus left with the case where
i=j
.

In particular, since
i=j
and
\mathtt{w}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
, by construction,
\langle\mathtt{w}_{i},\mathtt{r}_{i},\mathtt{w}_{j}\rangle
must be one of the following:

\displaystyle\langle\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}}),\mathtt{r}_{i}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}}),\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\rangle,\quad\langle\mathtt{w}_{i}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}}),\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}}),\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\rangle

\displaystyle\langle\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{y}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}}),\mathtt{r}_{i}({t^{\prime}_{y}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}}),\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{\alpha}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}})\rangle,\quad\langle\mathtt{w}_{i}({t^{\prime}_{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}}),\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}}),\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\rangle

\displaystyle\langle\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}),\mathtt{r}_{i}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}),\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}})\rangle,\quad\langle\mathtt{w}_{i}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}),\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}),\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}})\rangle

(21)

\displaystyle\langle\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{y}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}),\mathtt{r}_{i}({t^{\prime}_{y}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}),\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}})\rangle,\quad\langle\mathtt{w}_{i}({t^{\prime}_{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}),\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}),\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}})\rangle

We note two properties of each triplet
\langle\mathtt{w}_{i},\mathtt{r}_{i},\mathtt{w}_{j}\rangle
of
Eq.

21
.
First, either
\mathtt{r}_{i},\mathtt{w}_{j}\in\mathsf{Tid}_{x}
or
\mathtt{r}_{i},\mathtt{w}_{j}\in\mathsf{Tid}_{y}
.
Second,
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})
and
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{r}_{i})
are distinct verifier threads, and
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{j})
is a guesser thread.

Assume towards contradiction that
\mathtt{w}_{j}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}
.
By
Lemma

3.7
, there must exist a minimal
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path
\mathcal{P}\colon\mathtt{w}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{r}_{i}
.
Since
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{r}_{i}),\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{j})\in\mathsf{Tid}_{x}
or
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{r}_{i}),\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{j})\in\mathsf{Tid}_{y}
,
Lemma

3.8
guarantees that
\mathcal{P}
does not contain bridge events.
Next, observe that any
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path from
\mathtt{w}_{j}
to
\mathtt{r}_{i}
is of the form
\mathtt{w}_{j}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}^{?}$}}\mathtt{w}_{\ell}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{\ell}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}^{?}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}
, where
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell})
is a guesser thread and
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{r}_{\ell})
is a verifier thread.
Let
u=\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{i})=\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{r}_{i})=\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{j})
.
We consider the two cases, depending on whether
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell})=u
.

Case
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell})=u
.

If
\mathtt{r}_{i}=\mathtt{r}_{\ell}
, then
\mathtt{w}_{i}=\mathtt{w}_{\ell}
.
Since
\mathtt{w}_{i}\neq\mathtt{w}_{j}
,
Lemma

3.12
and
\mathtt{w}_{j}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i}
implies that
j<i
, which contradicts
j=i
.
Otherwise, since
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{r}_{\ell})=\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{r}_{i})
,
Lemma

3.10
implies that
\ell<i
.
We also have that
j\leq\ell
, since either
\mathtt{w}_{j}=\mathtt{w}_{\ell}
(so
j=\ell
), or

\mathtt{w}_{j}\neq\mathtt{w}_{\ell}
, and
Lemma

3.12
implies that
j<\ell
.
Combining the two inequalities, we arrive at
j<i
, contradicting that
j=i
.

Case
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell})\neq u
.

This case is more involved.
First, note that there are eight potential locations for
\mathtt{w}_{\ell}
, namely
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}}
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
, since these are the locations that connect a guesser thread to verifier thread via an
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edge.
Since
\mathtt{w}_{j}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{w}_{\ell}
, the location of
\mathtt{w}_{\ell}
is constrained by the thread topology in
Fig.

3
.
The guesser threads
{t_{\alpha}^{x}},{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{t_{\alpha}^{y}},
and
{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}}
, which write to locations
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}},
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}}
, have no
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-paths to the guesser threads
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}},{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{x}},{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}},
and
{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{y}}
, which write to locations
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}},
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
, and vice-versa.
Therefore,
u
and
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell})
belong to the same group, i.e., either
u,\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell})\in\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}}\}
or
u,\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell})\in\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}\}
.

Similarly, since
\mathtt{r}_{\ell}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}
, the location of
\mathtt{r}_{\ell}
is constrained by the thread topology in
Fig.

3
.
The verifier threads
{t_{x}}
and
t^{\prime}_{x}
, which write to locations
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
, have no
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-paths to the verifier threads in
{t_{y}}
and
{t^{\prime}_{y}}
, which write to locations
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
, and vice-versa.
Therefore,
u
and
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{r}_{\ell})
belong to the same group, i.e., either
u,\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{r}_{\ell})\in\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}\}
or
u,\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{r}_{\ell})\in\{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}\}
.
Since
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell})=\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{r}_{\ell})
, this constraint also applies to
\mathtt{w}_{\ell}
.
These two constraints together imply that both
u
and
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell})
are contained in exactly one of
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}}\}
,
\{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}}\}
,
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}\}
, or
\{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}\}
.
Since
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell})\neq u
, the pair
\langle\mathtt{w}_{j},\mathtt{w}_{\ell}\rangle
must be one of the following:

\displaystyle\footnotesize\langle\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}}),\mathtt{w}_{\ell}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\rangle,\hskip 2.12503pt\langle\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{\alpha}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}}),\mathtt{w}_{\ell}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\rangle,\hskip 2.12503pt

\displaystyle\langle\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}}),\mathtt{w}_{\ell}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\rangle,\hskip 2.5pt\langle\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}}),\mathtt{w}_{\ell}({t_{\alpha}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}})\rangle

(22)

\displaystyle\langle\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}),\mathtt{w}_{\ell}({{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}})\rangle,\hskip 2.5pt\langle\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}),\mathtt{w}_{\ell}({{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}})\rangle,\hskip 2.5pt

\displaystyle\langle\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}),\mathtt{w}_{\ell}({t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}})\rangle,\hskip 2.5pt\langle\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}),\mathtt{w}_{\ell}({t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}})\rangle

We split into two cases according to whether
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{j})
is the non-primed or primed location in this pair.
These correspond to the left and right columns of
Eq.

21
, respectively.

•

Non-primed locations (
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
).

We have
j<\ell
due to
Lemma

3.9
.
The path
\mathtt{r}_{\ell}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}^{?}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{r}_{i}
is composed of
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edges, read-to-write
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edges and a single read-to-read
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge, ending at
\mathtt{r}_{i}
.
The
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edges are non-decreasing by construction;
the read-to-write
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edges are increasing due to
Lemma

3.4
.
Finally, the final
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge cannot be any of the cases in
Lemma

3.5
, as these all end in primed locations, and
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{r}_{i})
is non-primed.
Therefore,
\ell\leq i
.
Combining the two inequalities, we arrive at
j<i
, contradicting that
j=i
.

•

Primed locations (
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
).

In these cases,
\langle\mathtt{w}_{j},\mathtt{w}_{\ell}\rangle
falls into one of the cases listed in
Lemma

3.11
.
Therefore,
j<\ell-1
.
The path
\mathtt{r}_{\ell}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}^{?}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{r}_{i}
is composed of
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edges, read-to-write
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edges and a single read-to-read
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge, ending at
\mathtt{r}_{i}
.
The
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edges are non-decreasing by construction;
the read-to-write
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edges are increasing due to
Lemma

3.4
.
Finally, by
Lemma

3.5
, the final
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge is decreasing by at most one;
overall, this yields
\ell-1\leq i
.
Combining the two inequalities, we arrive at
j<i
, contradicting that
j=i
.

4.
Decidability with Bounded Context Switches

In this section, we turn our attention to reachability under bounded context switches, and prove
Theorem

1.2
.
First, we consider the RMW-free fragment of Release/Acquire (
Sections

4.1
and
4.2
), since the main proof challenges arise already in this setting.
Then, we extend our proof to handle a bounded number of RMWs (
Section

4.3
).

Notation on event sequences.

Consider a sequence of events
\pi=e_{1},e_{2},\dots,e_{n}
.
Given some event
e
, we write
e\in\pi
to denote that
e=e_{i}
for some
i\in[n]
.
Given two events
e_{i},e_{j}\in\pi
, we write
e_{i}<_{\pi}e_{j}
to denote that
i<j
, and write
\leq_{\pi}
for the reflexive closure of
<_{\pi}
.
Finally, given two events
e_{i},e_{j}
with
e_{i}<_{\pi}e_{j}
, we define
\mathsf{range}_{\pi}(e_{i},e_{j})=\{e\in\pi\colon e_{i}<_{\pi}e\leq_{\pi}e_{j}\}
(note that containment is inclusive on the right endpoint only).

Contexts.

Consider a sequence of events
\pi=e_{1},e_{2},\dots,e_{n}
.
Given a thread
t
, a
t
-run
of
\pi
is a contiguous sub-sequence
e_{i+1},e_{i+2},\dots,e_{i+j}
of
\pi
such that
\mathsf{tid}(e_{i+\ell})=t
, for all
\ell\in[j]
.
We simply say that
e_{i+1},e_{i+2},\dots,e_{i+j}
is a
run
when
t
is not important, or clear from the context.
We say that the run is
maximal
if it is not a subsequence of another run of
\pi
.
Thus,
\pi
can be uniquely decomposed into a sequence
\pi=\pi_{1}\cdot\pi_{2}\cdots\pi_{\ell}
such that each
\pi_{i}
is a maximal run.
In such a case, we let
\mathsf{ctx}({\pi})={\ell}
be the
number of contexts
of
\pi
.
A
trace
is a pair
\mathcal{T}=\langle G,(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\dots,\pi_{\ell})\rangle
where

(i)

G
is an execution graph,

(ii)

\pi=\pi_{1}\cdot\pi_{2}\cdots\pi_{\ell}
is a topological ordering of
G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
, and

(iii)

each
\pi_{i}
is a maximal run of
\pi
.

Given an event
e\in G.E
, we write
\mathsf{cid}_{\mathcal{T}}(e)
for the unique
i\in[\ell]
such that
e\in\pi_{i}
.

Context-bounded reachability.

Given a concurrent program
\mathsf{P}
and a trace
\mathcal{T}=\langle G,(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\dots,\pi_{\ell})\rangle
, we
say that
\mathcal{T}
is a trace of
\mathsf{P}
if
G\in\llbracket{\mathsf{P}}\rrbracket
.
The problem of
context-bounded reachability
asks whether, given a concurrent program
\mathsf{P}
and some
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a trace
\mathcal{T}=\langle G,(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\dots,\pi_{\ell})\rangle
of
\mathsf{P}
, where
\ell\leq k
, that reaches the final state
\langle\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{1}}.\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{fn}},\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{2}}.\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{fn}},\dots,\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{{|\mathsf{Tid}|}}}.\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{fn}}\rangle
of
\mathsf{P}
.

4.1.
Reducible Traces

We first consider the RMW-free fragment of Release/Acquire.
The key insight is that, in the absence of RMWs, context-bounded reachability exhibits a small model property: if the final state of the program is indeed reachable, then it can be reached by an execution with size bounded by an exponential in the size of the program and the number of context switches.

We prove that reachability has small models by showing that, given a trace
\mathcal{T}=\langle G,(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\dots,\pi_{\ell})\rangle
witnessing reachability, if some run
\pi_{i}
is long enough, then it contains a segment that can be collapsed.
The challenge in this style of proofs is to identify, for each event
e\in\pi_{i}
, a bounded summary of the run up to
e
, which is sufficient to conclude that repeating summaries mark a collapsible segment.
In the current section, we define our event summaries, introduce some additional conditions under which repeating summaries mark a collapsible segment, indicating that the trace is
reducible
, and establish a bound on the size of irreducible traces.
In the next section we prove that reachability can be solved by only considering irreducible witnesses.

Event summaries.

Consider a program
\mathsf{P}
and a trace
\mathcal{T}=\langle G,(\pi_{1},\dots,\pi_{\ell})\rangle
of
\mathsf{P}
.
Consider some event
e\in G.E
, and let
t=\mathsf{tid}(e)
and
c=\mathsf{cid}_{\mathcal{T}}(e)
, for brevity.
Given some location
x\in\mathsf{Loc}
, we write
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e,x)
for the maximal, wrt
\leq_{\pi_{c}}
, write
\mathtt{w}(t,x)\in\pi_{c}
such that
\mathtt{w}(t,x)\leq_{\pi_{c}}e
.
If no such write exists, we let
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e,x)=\bot
.
We associate with
e
a
summary

\mathsf{Sum}_{\mathcal{T}}(e)=\langle Q,\phi,\mathsf{Erf}\rangle
, where each component is defined as follows.

•

Q\subseteq\mathsf{Pseq}_{t}.\mathcal{Q}
is the set of states that
\mathsf{Pseq}_{t}
reaches by executing all events of thread
t
up to (including)
e
.
Formally, let
e_{i},e_{j},\dots,e_{n}
be the (unique) prefix of
G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}^{t}
up to
e_{n}=e
.
Then
q\in Q
iff
\mathsf{lab}(e_{i}),\mathsf{lab}(e_{j}),\dots,\mathsf{lab}(e_{n})
reaches
q
in
\mathsf{Pseq}_{t}
.

•

\phi\colon\mathsf{Loc}\to\mathsf{Val}\cup\{\bot\}
is a map from each location
x
to the value of the most recent write of thread
t
to
x
in
\pi_{c}
.
Formally,
\phi(x)=\mathsf{val}_{w}(\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e,x))
, if
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e,x)\neq\bot
, and
\phi(x)=\bot
otherwise.

•

\mathsf{Erf}\subseteq\mathsf{Loc}
is the set of locations that
t
reads from a remote thread between its most recent local write on said location in
\pi_{c}
and
e
.
Formally,
x\in\mathsf{Erf}
iff
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e,x)\neq\bot
and there exists a read
\mathtt{r}(t,x)\in\mathsf{range}_{\pi_{c}}(\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e,x),e)
such that
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e,x)\not\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize\hskip 5.0pt$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}(t,x)
.

Reducible traces.

Consider a trace
\mathcal{T}=\langle G,(\pi_{1},\dots,\pi_{\ell})\rangle
.
Two events
e_{i},e_{j}\in G.E
are called
collapsible
if
e_{i}<_{\pi}e_{j}
and, letting
c=\mathsf{cid}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i})
, the following conditions hold:

(i)

\mathsf{cid}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j})=c
,

(ii)

\mathsf{Sum}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i})=\mathsf{Sum}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j})
,

(iii)

for every write
\mathtt{w}(t,x)\in\mathsf{range}_{\pi_{c}}(e_{i},e_{j})
, if
\mathtt{w}(t,x)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}e
, for some event
e\in G.E
, then
\mathsf{cid}_{\mathcal{T}}(e)=c
, and

(iv)

for every
x\in\mathsf{Loc}
and
e\in(G.E\setminus G.E^{\mathsf{tid}(e_{i})})
, we have
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i},x)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}e
iff
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j},x)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}e
.

Intuitively,
e_{i}
and
e_{j}
have the same context id and summary,
there is no write between them that is observed by a later run, and
the latest write on each location reaches the same set of events in other threads via
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
.
We call
\mathcal{T}

reducible
if it contains a collapsible pair of events, and
irreducible
otherwise.
The following lemma gives a bound on the size of irreducible traces.

Lemma 4.1.

For any concurrent program
\mathsf{P}
and trace
\mathcal{T}=\langle G,(\pi_{1},\dots,\pi_{\ell})\rangle
of
\mathsf{P}
,
if
\mathcal{T}
is irreducible then
|G.E|=O(2^{\ell\cdot|\mathsf{P}.\mathcal{Q}|})
.

4.2.
A Small Model Property

The small model property of reachability in the RMW-free fragment follows from
Lemma

4.1
and the following lemma, proved in this section.

Lemma 4.2.

If there is a trace
\langle G,(\pi_{1},\pi_{2}\dots,\pi_{\ell})\rangle
of
\mathsf{P}
reaching a state
\langle p_{1},p_{2},\dots,p_{{|\mathsf{Tid}|}}\rangle
,
then there exists an irreducible trace with the same property.

G\to G^{\prime}
\pi_{c}
\pi_{c}^{\prime}
\cdots
e_{i}
\cdots
e_{j}
\cdots
\cdots
e_{i}
\cdots

(a)

G
and
G^{\prime}

G\to G^{\prime}
\pi_{c}
\pi_{c}^{\prime}
\mathtt{w}_{i}
\cdots
\cdots
\mathtt{r}
\mathtt{w}_{i}
\cdots
\cdots
\mathtt{w}
\cdots
\cdots
\mathtt{r}

(b)

G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
and
G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}

G\to G^{\prime}
\pi_{c}
\pi_{c}^{\prime}
\mathtt{w}_{i}
\cdots
\cdots
\mathtt{w}_{j}
\cdots
\cdots
\mathtt{w}
\mathtt{w}
\mathtt{w}
\mathtt{w}_{i}
\cdots
\cdots
\mathtt{w}
\mathtt{w}
\mathtt{w}

(c)

G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
and
G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}

Figure 7
.
A graph
G
with
\mathsf{Sum}(e_{i})=\mathsf{Sum}(e_{j})
and the corresponding
G^{\prime}
(
7(a)
).
(
7(b)
) illustrates the recovery of missing
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
edges, and
(
7(c)
) illustrates how
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
is adapted to restore
for the recovered
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
.

Reducing
\langle G,(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\dots,\pi_{\ell})\rangle
.

Assume that
\langle G,(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\dots,\pi_{\ell})\rangle
is reducible via a collapsible pair of events
e_{i},e_{j}
, and let
t=\mathsf{tid}(e_{i})=\mathsf{tid}(e_{j})
and
c=\mathsf{cid}(e_{i})=\mathsf{cid}(e_{j})
.
We construct a new trace
\langle G^{\prime},(\pi^{\prime}_{1},\pi^{\prime}_{2},\dots,\pi^{\prime}_{\ell})\rangle
, such that
G^{\prime}.E\subset G.E
and
G^{\prime}
also reaches
\langle p_{1},p_{2},\dots,p_{{|\mathsf{Tid}|}}\rangle
.
Since reachability witnesses are finite,
Lemma

4.2
then follows.

In the following we describe the construction of
G^{\prime}
in three steps.
Each step is illustrated in
Fig.

7
.
The sequence
(\pi^{\prime}_{1},\pi^{\prime}_{2},\dots,\pi^{\prime}_{\ell})
is obtained naturally, by removing from each
\pi_{l}
the events that do not appear in
G^{\prime}.E
.

1. Collapsing the segment between
e_{i}
and
e_{j}
.

We obtain the event set
G^{\prime}.E
by removing from
G
all events between
e_{i}
(exclusive) and
e_{j}
(inclusive), i.e.,
G^{\prime}E=G.E\setminus\mathsf{range}_{\pi_{c}}(e_{i},e_{j})
.
The program order
G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
is
G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
restricted to
G^{\prime}.E
, i.e.,
G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}=G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}\cap(G^{\prime}.E\times G^{\prime}.E)
.
See
Fig.

7(a)
for an illustration.

2. Recovering missing
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edges.

The reads-from relation between all pairs of events that are present in both
G
and
G^{\prime}
is preserved, i.e.,
if
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}
and
\mathtt{w},\mathtt{r}\in G^{\prime}.E
then
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}
.
Now, consider an
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edge
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}
for which
\mathtt{r}\in G^{\prime}.E
but
\mathtt{w}\not\in G^{\prime}.E
.
Then, we have to pair
\mathtt{r}
with a new writer in
G^{\prime}
.
Since
\langle e_{i},e_{j}\rangle
form a collapsible pair, we have
\mathtt{w}\in\mathsf{range}(e_{i},e_{j})
, and thus
\mathsf{cid}(\mathtt{r})=c
.
Let
x=\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{r})
.
Since
\mathsf{Sum}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i})=\mathsf{Sum}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j})
, there is some value
v\in\mathsf{Val}
such that
\mathsf{Sum}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i}).\phi(x)=\mathsf{Sum}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j}).\phi(x)=v
.
We set
\mathtt{w}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}
, where
\mathtt{w}_{i}=\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i},x)
.
See
Fig.

7(b)
for an illustration.

3. Redirecting
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
-edges.

With new
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edges in place, we may have to adapt
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
in order to restore
.
In particular, consider some location
x\in\mathsf{Loc}
.
If
\mathsf{Sum}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i}).\phi(x)=\bot
or
x\in\mathsf{Sum}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i}).\mathsf{Erf}
, we set
G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}_{x}=G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}_{x}\cap(G^{\prime}.E\times G^{\prime}.E)
.
Otherwise, let
\mathtt{w}_{i}=\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i},x)
and
\mathtt{w}_{j}=\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j},x)
.
We set
G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}_{x}=\overline{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}_{x}}\cap(G^{\prime}.E\times G^{\prime}.E)
, where
\overline{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}_{x}}
is identical to
G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}_{x}
, with
\mathtt{w}_{i}
and
\mathtt{w}_{j}
swapped.
See
Fig.

7(c)
for an illustration.
