Figure 8
.

The graph
G\in\llbracket{\mathsf{P}}\rrbracket
can be reduced to
G^{\prime}
while still reaching the final state of
\mathsf{P}
. This is done without increasing the number of contexts of
G
, by simply removing events in
G
and changing
G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
and
G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
.

Fig.

8
shows a concrete example of witness reduction.
Since the
\mathsf{Sum}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i}).Q=\mathsf{Sum}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j}).Q
, it follows easily that
G^{\prime}
represents a valid execution of
\mathsf{P}
that reaches the same set of states as
G
.
The intricate part of the proof is in showing that
G^{\prime}

is Release/Acquire-consistent, and thus
G^{\prime}\in\llbracket{\mathsf{P}}\rrbracket
.

The consistency of
G^{\prime}
.

We argue that
G^{\prime}
satisfies each of the Release/Acquire axioms of
Fig.

1
, besides
, since
G^{\prime}
is RMW-free.

.

Note that
G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}\subseteq G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
, while
G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}\setminus G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}\subseteq G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
.
This implies that
G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}\subseteq G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
, and since
G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
is irreflexive, the same holds for
G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
.

.

Since
G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}\subseteq G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
, a potential violation of
in
G^{\prime}
must include an edge from
(G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}\setminus G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo})
.
Any such edge has the form
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i}
, where
\mathtt{w}_{i}=\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i},x)
for some location
x
.
By construction, this implies

(i)

\mathtt{w}_{i}=\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i},x)
,

(ii)

\mathtt{w}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}
, and

(iii)

\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
,

where
\mathtt{w}_{j}=\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j},x)
and
x=\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w})=\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{i})
.
Note that this ordering is only possible if
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w})\neq\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})
, as all writes on
x
in the same thread between
\mathtt{w}_{i}
(exclusive) and
\mathtt{w}_{j}
(inclusive) have been removed in
G^{\prime}
.
Since
G
satisfies
, we have
\mathtt{w}_{j}\not\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize\hskip 5.0pt$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{w}
.
Moreover, since
\langle e_{i},e_{j}\rangle
is collapsible, for any event
e
such that
\mathsf{tid}(e)\neq\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})
, we have that
\mathtt{w}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}e
iff
\mathtt{w}_{j}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}e
.
Therefore,
\mathtt{w}_{j}\not\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize\hskip 5.0pt$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{w}
implies
\mathtt{w}_{i}\not\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize\hskip 5.0pt$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{w}
.
Since
G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}\subseteq G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
, we also have
\mathtt{w}_{i}\not\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize\hskip 5.0pt$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{w}
, and hence the edge
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i}
does not participate in a
violation in
G^{\prime}
.

.

A violation of
in
G^{\prime}
is witnessed by a triplet of distinct events
\langle\mathtt{w},\mathtt{r},\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\rangle
accessing the same location
x
and such that

(i)

\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}
,

(ii)

\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}
, and

(iii)

\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}^{\prime}

We argue that any triplet
\langle\mathtt{w},\mathtt{r},\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\rangle
fails one of
Items

(i)
,
(ii)
and
(iii)
.
We consider two cases, depending on whether
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}
, i.e., whether the writer of
\mathtt{r}
is the same in
G
and
G^{\prime}
.

G\to G^{\prime}
\pi
\pi^{\prime}
\mathtt{r}^{\ref{item:vio_1}}
\mathtt{w}_{i}
\mathtt{r}^{\ref{item:vio_2}}
\cdots
\mathtt{w}_{j}
\cdots
\mathtt{r}^{\ref{item:vio_3}}
\mathtt{w}
\mathtt{r}^{\ref{item:vio_1}}
\mathtt{w}_{i}
\mathtt{r}^{\ref{item:vio_2}}
\mathtt{r}^{\ref{item:vio_3}}
\mathtt{w}

(a)

The case of
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.35623pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}
.

\pi

\mathtt{w}^{\prime}

(b)

The case of
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.35623pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}\setminus G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}
.

Figure 9
.

The two cases in the proof of
for
G^{\prime}
on a triplet
\langle\mathtt{w},\mathtt{r},\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\rangle
depending on whether
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.35623pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}
(
9(a)
) or
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.35623pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}\setminus G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}
(
9(b)
).

The case of
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}
.

Recall that
G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}\subseteq G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
, hence if
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}
, we also have
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}
.
Since also
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}
and
G
satisfies
,
we have
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}
.
Assume towards contradiction that
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}^{\prime}
, and by construction, we have
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}=\mathtt{w}_{i}
, where
\mathtt{w}_{i}=\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i},x)
.
Let
\pi^{\prime}=\pi^{\prime}_{1}\cdot\pi^{\prime}_{2}\cdots\pi^{\prime}_{\ell}
(recall that
\langle G^{\prime},(\pi^{\prime}_{1},\pi^{\prime}_{2},\dots,\pi^{\prime}_{\ell})\rangle
is the trace we have constructed).
We consider where
\mathtt{r}
appears in
\pi^{\prime}
, relatively to
\mathtt{w}_{i}
and
e_{i}
(see also
Fig.

9(a)
).

(1)

\mathtt{r}<_{\pi^{\prime}}\mathtt{w}_{i}
.
This is not possible, since
\pi^{\prime}
is a total extension of
G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
and we have
\mathtt{w}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}
.

(2)

\mathtt{w}_{i}<_{\pi^{\prime}}\mathtt{r}\leq_{\pi^{\prime}}e_{i}
.
Note that, in this case,
\mathsf{cid}(\mathtt{r})=c
, i.e.,
\mathtt{r}
belongs to the same thread as
\mathtt{w}_{i}
and
e_{i}
, and thus
\mathtt{w}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{r}
.
Since
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}\setminus G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i}
, the condition for redirecting
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
-edges for location
x
must have been triggered in the reduction of
\langle G,(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\dots,\pi_{\ell})\rangle
.
This is only possible if
x\not\in\mathsf{Sum}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i}).\mathsf{Erf}
.
This, in turn, implies that
\mathtt{r}
reads from a local write in
G
, i.e.,
\mathtt{w}=\mathtt{w}_{i}
, contradicting the requirement that
\mathtt{w}
and
\mathtt{w}_{i}
are distinct.

(3)

e_{j}<_{\pi^{\prime}}\mathtt{r}
.
Since
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}\setminus G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}^{\prime}
, we have
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
, where
\mathtt{w}_{j}=\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j},x)
.
Since
G
satisfies
, we have
\mathtt{w}_{j}\not\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize\hskip 5.0pt$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}
.
Since
\mathtt{w}_{j}<_{\pi}e_{j}<_{\pi}\mathtt{r}
, this implies that
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{r})\neq\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{j})
, or equivalently,
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{r})\neq\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})
, as otherwise we would have
\mathtt{w}_{j}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{r}
and thus
\mathtt{w}_{j}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}
.
By the collapsibility of
\langle e_{i},e_{j}\rangle
, for any event
e
such that
\mathsf{tid}(e)\neq\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})
, we have that
\mathtt{w}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}e
iff
\mathtt{w}_{j}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}e
.
Therefore,
\mathtt{w}_{j}\not\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize\hskip 5.0pt$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}
implies
\mathtt{w}_{i}\not\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize\hskip 5.0pt$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}
.
Finally, since
G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}\subseteq G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
, we obtain
\mathtt{w}_{i}\not\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize\hskip 5.0pt$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}
.

Hence, every triplet
\langle\mathtt{w},\mathtt{r},\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\rangle
of
G^{\prime}
with
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}
satisfies
.

The case of
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}\setminus G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}
.

Any edge in
(G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}\setminus G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf})
is of the form
\mathtt{w}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
, where
\mathtt{w}_{i}=\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i},x)
and
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j},x)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
.
We thus have
\mathtt{w}=\mathtt{w}_{i}
and
\mathtt{r}=\mathtt{r}_{j}
.
Moreover, let
\mathtt{w}_{j}=\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j},x)
be the original writer of
\mathtt{r}_{j}
in
G
.
Since
G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}\subseteq G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
, if
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
, we also have
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
, and as
G
satisfies
, we have
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
.
We argue that
\mathsf{Sum}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i}).\phi(x)\neq\bot
and
x\not\in\mathsf{Sum}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i}).\mathsf{Erf}
, which are the conditions under which
\mathtt{w}_{i}
takes the place of
\mathtt{w}_{j}
in
G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
, establishing that
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i}
, as illustrated in
Fig.

9(b)
.

The presence of
\mathtt{w}_{j}
establishes that
\mathsf{Sum}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i}).\phi(x)\neq\bot
.
Moreover, if
x\in\mathsf{Sum}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i}).\mathsf{Erf}
, there would be a read event
\mathtt{r}^{\prime\prime}
, also on location
x
, such that

(i)

\mathtt{w}_{j}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{r}^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}e_{j}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
, and

(ii)

\mathtt{w}^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}^{\prime\prime}
, where
\mathtt{w}^{\prime\prime}\neq\mathtt{w}^{\prime}
.

This, however, would imply a violation of read coherence for
G
, witnessed either by the triplet
\langle\mathtt{w}_{j},\mathtt{r}_{j},\mathtt{w}^{\prime\prime}\rangle
(if
\mathtt{w}_{j}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}^{\prime\prime}
), or
\langle\mathtt{w}^{\prime\prime},\mathtt{r}^{\prime\prime},\mathtt{w}_{j}\rangle
(if
\mathtt{w}^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
).

Hence, every triplet
\langle\mathtt{w},\mathtt{r},\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\rangle
of
G^{\prime}
with
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}\setminus G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}
also satisfies
, concluding the proof of the consistency of
G^{\prime}
.

4.3.
Small Models with Bounded RMWs

Finally, we address the case of reachability as witnessed by traces that contain both bounded context switches and bounded RMWs, thereby concluding
Theorem

1.2
.

G\to G^{\prime}
\pi
\pi^{\prime}
\mathtt{w}_{i}
\cdots
\cdots

RMWs prevent the collapsibility of segments between repeating summaries.

Where does the proof above break with RMWs?

To gain proof intuition, it is natural to ask why our construction in the previous section fails in the presence of RMWs.

Fig.

10
illustrates this case.
In particular, recall that our construction works as follows (see also
Fig.

10(a)
): if there is a segment between two collapsible events
e_{i}
and
e_{j}
containing a write
\mathtt{w}_{j}
that is read by a read
\mathtt{r}_{j}
after
e_{j}
, in
G^{\prime}
we identify another write
\mathtt{w}_{i}
which

(i)

becomes the writer of
\mathtt{r}_{j}
, and

(ii)

receives all the
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
edges of
\mathtt{w}_{j}
in
G
, in order to restore
for
\mathtt{r}_{j}
relative to other conflicting writes
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}
for which
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
.

Now consider the case that
\mathtt{w}_{i}
is replaced by an RMW
\mathtt{rmw}_{i}
,
reading from another write
\mathtt{w}^{\prime\prime}
for which also
\mathtt{w}^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}^{\prime}
(see also
Fig.

10(b)
).
Performing the same sequence of changes, namely
\mathtt{rmw}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
and
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{rmw}_{i}
, would violate
, as
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}
is
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
-between
\mathtt{rmw}_{i}
and its writer.
Moreover, the
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
order from
\mathtt{w}^{\prime\prime}
to
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}
might not be reversible, e.g., because
\mathtt{w}^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{w}^{\prime}
.
In the following, we lift the proof of the previous section to handle executions with a bounded number of RMWs.

Context-bounded reachability with RMWs.

The problem of
context-bounded reachability with bounded RMWs
asks whether, given a concurrent program
\mathsf{P}
and parameters
k_{c},k_{\mathtt{rmw}}\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a trace
\langle G,(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\dots,\pi_{\ell})\rangle
of
\mathsf{P}
with
\ell\leq k_{c}
and
|(G.E\cap\mathsf{RMW})|\leq k_{\mathtt{rmw}}
that reaches the final state
\langle\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{1}}.\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{fn}},\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{2}}.\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{fn}},\dots,\mathsf{Pseq}_{t_{{|\mathsf{Tid}|}}}.\mathsf{q}_{\mathsf{fn}}\rangle
of
\mathsf{P}
.

Reducible traces with RMWs.

To complete our proof of
Theorem

1.2
, we extend the definition of reducible traces as follows.
First, we redefine
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i},x)
to properly handle RMW events:
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e,x)
is now the maximal
writing
(i.e., either a plain write or an RMW) event
e^{\prime}(t,x)
with respect to
\leq_{\pi_{c}}
such that
e^{\prime}(t,x)\leq_{\pi_{c}}e
.
Second, we deem two events
e_{i}
and
e_{j}
collapsible if, in addition to
Items

(i)
,
(ii)
,
(iii)
and
(iv)
, they satisfy the following condition:

(v)

for all
x\in\mathsf{Loc}
, if
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i},x)\neq\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j},x)
, then
\mathsf{op}(\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i},x))=\mathtt{w}
.

When reducing a trace, this condition prevents inserting
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edges from RMWs, and thus avoids the need to further insert
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
edges to RMWs, thereby avoiding the offending case of
Fig.

10(b)
.
The following lemma bounds the size of irreducible traces, generalizing
Lemma

4.1
.

Lemma 4.3.

For any concurrent program
\mathsf{P}
and trace
\mathcal{T}=\langle G,(\pi_{1},\dots,\pi_{\ell})\rangle
of
\mathsf{P}
,
if
\mathcal{T}
is irreducible then
|G.E|=O(2^{\ell\cdot(|\mathsf{P}.\mathcal{Q}|+|G.E\cap\mathsf{RMW}|)})
.

Finally, given a reducible trace
\langle G,(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\dots,\pi_{\ell})\rangle
, we construct a trace
\langle G^{\prime},(\pi^{\prime}_{1},\pi^{\prime}_{2},\dots,\pi^{\prime}_{\ell})\rangle
by following the process described in
Section

4.2
.
It remains to argue that
G^{\prime}
is consistent.

The consistency of
G^{\prime}
.

The proof for
,
and
remains the same as in
Section

4.2
.
We further need to argue that
G^{\prime}
does not violate
.
A violation is witnessed by a triplet of distinct events
\langle\mathtt{w},\mathtt{rmw},\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\rangle
such that

(i)

\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{rmw}
,

(ii)

\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{rmw}
, and

(iii)

\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}^{\prime}
.

We argue that any triplet
\langle\mathtt{w},\mathtt{rmw},\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\rangle
that satisfies

Item

(i)
and
Item

(ii)
is such that
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}
, i.e., it fails
Item

(iii)
.
Due to
Item

(v)
of collapsibility, no
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
-edges pointing to RMWs are introduced in
G^{\prime}
, thus
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{rmw}
.
We consider two cases, depending on whether
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{rmw}
, i.e., whether the writer of
\mathtt{rmw}
is the same in
G
and
G^{\prime}
.

The case of
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{rmw}
.

Since also
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{rmw}
and
G
satisfies
, we have
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}
.
For the last relation to be reversed in
G^{\prime}
, i.e.,
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}^{\prime}
, it follows by construction that
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}=\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i},x)
and there is some removed event
\mathtt{w}_{j}=\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j},x)
such that
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
.
Since
G
satisfies
, we have
\mathtt{rmw}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
.
In this case, however, the construction also forces
\mathtt{rmw}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}^{\prime}
, a contradiction.

The case of
\mathtt{w}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}\setminus G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{rmw}
.

Observe that, in this case, we have
\mathtt{w}=\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i},x)
and
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j},x)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}_{x}$}}\mathtt{rmw}
.
Let
\mathtt{w}_{j}=\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j},x)
be the original writer of
\mathtt{rmw}
.
As already argued in the proof of read coherence of
Section

4.2
,

\mathtt{w}
also obtains in
G^{\prime}
the
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}
predecessors of
\mathtt{w}_{j}
in
G
.
Since
\langle\mathtt{w}_{j},\mathtt{rmw},\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\rangle
does not violate
in
G
, we have
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
, and thus also
\mathtt{w}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$G^{\prime}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}

We thus obtain that
G^{\prime}
satisfies
, as desired.

5.
Related Work

The decidability of verification, typically instantiated in its most basic form of state reachability, has been a subject of systematic study under weak memory.
In
(
6
;
7
)
, it was shown that
\mathsf{TSO}
and
\mathsf{PSO}
admit decidable verification, but the problem becomes undecidable when allowing read to read/write reorderings.
Verification was recently shown to be undecidable under Power
(
3
)
.
Release/Acquire semantics have also been studied in weak memory under close variants, namely Strong/Weak/Localized Release/Acquire
(
17
;
26
)
, and shown to all admit decidable verification.
This indicates that the undecidability of standard Release/Acquire depends intricately on the specifics of this model,
and may partly explain why the undecidability of RMW-free programs has been open for this long.

A significant body of work has focused on analyzing concurrent programs under some sort of context bounding.
This setting has been practically utilized for programs under Sequential Consistency
(
23
;
19
;
28
)
, and its computational complexity has been well-characterized
(
15
;
13
;
11
)
.
Bounded context switches have also been employed in TSO
(
8
;
9
)
and Power
(
4
)
.
Since undecidability under Release/Acquire with RMWs already holds for executions with just 4 contexts,
(
2
)
developed the stronger restriction of “view switches”, and showed that decidability is recovered under bounded view switches.

6.
Conclusion

We have proven that the reachability problem of concurrent programs under Release/Acquire semantics is undecidable even for RMW-free programs, resolving an open question of
(
2
)
.
Moreover, we have shown that RMW-free programs enjoy decidable verification under bounded context switches, in contrast to programs with RMWs, for which undecidability already holds with 4-context executions.
Interesting future work includes establishing tight complexity bounds (though not stated explicitly, our proofs imply that the problem lies between
\mathsf{NP}
and
\mathsf{NEXP}
).

Proof of
Eqs.

15

16
.

These invariants are established in pairs, starting with

\mathtt{w}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}}),\quad\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})\ ,

and generally, each pair involves writes from a thread
t
read by thread
t^{\prime}
and vice versa.
The proof of each pair has identical structure, and is based on the fact that each of the involved threads alternates between writing to one of the involved locations and reading from the other.
To avoid redundancy, we only argue about the first pair.
Our proof follows an induction on
i
.

First, observe that
\mathtt{w}_{1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
.
This holds because
\mathtt{r}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
must read value
1
.
Any later write
\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
that writes this value is such that
j\geq 5
.
However, any such
\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
is preceded by
\mathtt{r}_{4}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})
, which reads value
0
.
In turn, this value is written by writes of
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
that appear after
\mathtt{r}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
.
Hence, if
\mathtt{r}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
were to read from any write
\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
with
j\geq 2
, this would cause an
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-cycle, violating
.
Symmetric reasoning on
\mathtt{r}_{1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})
establishes that
\mathtt{w}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})
.

The argument proceeds inductively.
In particular,
\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
cannot read from any
\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
, with
j<i
, because

(i)

For all
j<i-1
,
\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i-1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
,

(ii)

by the induction hypothesis, we have
\mathtt{w}_{i-1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i-1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
and thus
\mathtt{w}_{i-1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
, and

(iii)

\mathsf{val}_{\mathtt{w}}(\mathtt{w}_{i-1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}}))\neq\mathsf{val}_{\mathtt{r}}(\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}}))
.

Therefore, reading from any
\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
that writes
\mathsf{val}_{\mathtt{r}}(\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}}))
, with
j<i
, would violate
.

Moreover, any later write
\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
with
\mathsf{val}_{\mathtt{w}}(\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}}))=\mathsf{val}_{\mathtt{r}}(\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}}))
is such that
j\geq i+4
.
However, any such
\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
is preceded by
\mathtt{r}_{i+2}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})
.
In turn,
\mathtt{r}_{i+2}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})
must read its value from a write that appears after
\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
in
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
.
This is because any earlier write that writes
\mathsf{val}_{\mathtt{r}}(\mathtt{r}_{i+2}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}}))
would be of the form
\mathtt{w}_{\ell}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})
, with
\ell<i-1
, and, by the induction hypothesis, we have that
\mathtt{w}_{i-1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i-1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})
; therefore,
\mathtt{r}_{i+2}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})
reading from
\mathtt{w}_{\ell}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})
would violate
.
This implies that
\mathtt{r}_{i+2}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})
must read its value from some write later than
\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})
in
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
, and thus also later than
\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
.
Hence, if
\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
with

, we would have a violation of
.
We thus arrive at
\mathtt{w}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
, as desired.
Symmetric reasoning on
\mathtt{r}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})
establishes that
\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})
.

The desired result follows.
