, we have that
\mathtt{w}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})
, which implies that
\mathtt{w}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
for all
i\geq 2
.
If
\mathtt{w}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{1}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
for any
i\geq 2
, then we would have
\mathtt{w}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{1}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
.
Since, by
Eq.

16
, we have
\mathtt{w}_{1}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{1}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
, together with
\mathtt{w}_{1}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{1}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
, this would create a violation of
.
We thus have
\mathtt{w}_{1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{1}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
.

The argument then repeats inductively.
In particular,
\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
cannot read from any
\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
with
j<i
, because, by the induction hypothesis, we have
\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{j}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
, while by construction, we have
\mathtt{r}_{j}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
.
These imply that
\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
, and thus
\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
due to
.
Together with
\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
, the two facts
\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
and
\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
would violate
.
Moreover, since
\mathtt{w}_{i-1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i-1}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
by the induction hypothesis,
and also
\mathtt{w}_{i-1}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i-1}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
, we also have
\mathtt{w}_{i-1}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i-1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
due to
.
Due to
Eq.

15
, we have that
\mathtt{w}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
,
which implies that
\mathtt{w}_{i-1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
for all
j>i
.
If
\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
for any
j>i
, then we would have
\mathtt{w}_{i-1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i-1}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
.
Since, by the induction hypothesis, we have
\mathtt{w}_{i-1}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i-1}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
, together with
\mathtt{w}_{i-1}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i-1}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
, this would violate
.
We thus have
\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
.

Finally, we argue that
\mathtt{w}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
.
In particular,
\mathtt{r}_{i}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
cannot read from any
\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
with
j<i
, because, by the induction hypothesis, we have
\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
, while by construction, we have
\mathtt{r}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
.
These imply that
\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
, and thus
\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
due to
.
Together with
\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
, the two facts
\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
and
\mathtt{w}_{i}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
would violate
.
Moreover, since
\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
and
\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
, this implies that
\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
, due to
.
Due to
Eq.

15
, we have that
\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}})
, which implies that
\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
for all
j>i
.
If
\mathtt{w}_{j}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
for any
j>i
, then we would have
\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
.
Since, as previously established,
\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
, together with
\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}mo}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
, this would violate
.
We thus have
\mathtt{w}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{i}(t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
.

The desired result follows.
∎

A.2.
Proofs of
Section

3.4

Here we prove the auxiliary lemmas of
Section

3.4
.

See
3.4

Proof.

We analyze the
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
of different threads separately.
Let an
iteration
be a sequence of events corresponding to one execution of the main loop of each thread, as presented in
Fig.

6

; for
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
and
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}
, we use the innermost loop.

Threads
{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}
,
{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}}
,
{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{x}}
,
{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{y}}
,
t^{\prime}_{x}
and
{t^{\prime}_{y}}
.

Each iteration in these threads executes its respective events exactly once and in the same order.
Furthermore, no location is written to or read from twice in the same iteration.
As a consequence, the indices of all events increase by exactly one in each iteration, and, in these threads,
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
is naturally non-decreasing (satisfying
Item

(i)
);
moreover, since each iteration in these threads executes a sequence of writes followed by a sequence of reads, a write event succeeding a read event cannot be in its same iteration, therefore every read-to-write
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge is increasing (satisfying
Item

(ii)
).

Threads
{t_{x}}
,
{t_{y}}
,
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
and
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}
.

These threads skip a suffix of reads in the first iteration.
Otherwise, its respective events are executed exactly once and in the same order and no location is written to or read from twice in the same iteration .
For instance, in
{t_{x}}
, the
(i+1)
-th iteration contains
\mathtt{r}_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
and
\mathtt{r}_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}})
rather than
\mathtt{r}_{i+1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
and
\mathtt{r}_{i+1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}})
.
However, all writes in these threads succeeding the
(i+1)
-th iteration are still of the form
\mathtt{w}_{j}
for
i+1<j
.
Since any
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge from a read in the
i+1
-th iteration to such a write satisfies
i+1<j
,
i<j
, and therefore
Item

(ii)
holds.

Threads
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
and
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}
.

We only consider
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
, as the case for
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}
is symmetric.
Let us first consider edges
e_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
that do not include events on
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}}
.
In this case, we are in the same scenario as
{t_{x}}
,
{t_{y}}
,
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
and
{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}
, where a suffix of reads (namely, a read on
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}}
) is skipped in the first iteration.
As argued in the paragraph above,
Item

(i)
and
Item

(ii)
hold.

Let us now consider edges that do include events on
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}}
.
Since these events are not non-bridge writes (and therefore candidates for
\mathtt{w}_{j}
), we only need to consider the cases
\mathtt{w}_{i}({\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
and
\mathtt{r}_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
.

Case
\mathtt{w}_{i}({\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
.

The inner loop of
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
always executes at least once after an event
\mathtt{w}_{i}({\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}})
;
therefore, this event precedes the
j^{\prime}
-th iteration, where
j^{\prime}\geq i
.
Since all non-bridge writes of
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
are located in its inner loop, all non-bridge writes
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-ordered after
\mathtt{w}_{i}({\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}})
must all have index
j\geq j^{\prime}\geq i
, satisfying
Item

(i)
.

Case
\mathtt{r}_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
.

The case above shows that
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edges connecting a bridge write to a non-bridge writes are non-decreasing.
Therefore, all non-bridge writes
\mathtt{w}_{j}
such that
\mathtt{w}_{i+1}({\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
are such that
j\geq i+1
.
Since
\mathtt{r}_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}})
immediately precedes
\mathtt{w}_{i+1}({\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}})
in
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-order (by inspection of
Fig.

6
),
all non-bridge writes
\mathtt{w}_{j}
such that
\mathtt{r}_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}

are such that
j\geq i+1
, satisfying
Item

(ii)
.
∎

See
3.5

Proof.

Since same-location
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edges between read events must be increasing by definition,
we only need to consider the threads with non-bridge reads on more than one locations.
These are
{t_{x}}
,
t^{\prime}_{x}
,
{t_{y}}
and
{t^{\prime}_{y}}
.
Let an
iteration
be a sequence of events corresponding to one execution of the main loop of each thread, as presented in
Fig.

6
.

Threads
t^{\prime}_{x}
and
{t^{\prime}_{y}}
.

These threads execute the same read events exactly once in each iteration, with no location being read from twice in the same iteration.
Therefore the indices of their events are, overall, non-decreasing.

Threads
{t_{x}}
and
{t_{y}}
.

Thread
{t_{x}}
skips reads on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
in the first iteration.
Otherwise, each iteration in these threads executes its respective events exactly once and in the same order.
Furthermore, no location is written to or read from twice in the same iteration.
This means that, from the second iteration of thread
{t_{x}}
onward, the two reads
\mathtt{r}_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
and
\mathtt{r}_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}})
are succeeded by reads
\mathtt{r}_{i-1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}})
and
\mathtt{r}_{i-1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}})
.
The four corresponding pairs
\langle\mathtt{r}_{i},\mathtt{r}_{i-1}\rangle
yield the stated decreasing edges.

Thread
{t_{y}}
follows the same construction, but with locations
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}}
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
, replacing
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
, respectively.
∎

See
3.7

Proof.

Consider any alternating
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path
\mathcal{P}\colon a\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}b
.
If
\mathcal{P}
is already minimal, we are done.
Otherwise,
\mathcal{P}
contains two
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edges on the same thread, i.e., it has the form

a\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}^{?}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}c\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}d\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}^{?}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}e\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}f\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}^{?}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}b\ .

where
\mathsf{tid}(c)=\mathsf{tid}(f)
.
We argue that
c\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}f
; otherwise, we have
f\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}c
, and thus
f\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}c\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}d\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}^{?}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}e\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}f
is an
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-cycle, contradicting
Lemma

3.6
.
We can therefore replace the sub-path from
c
to
f
by the single
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge
c\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}f
, yielding

a\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}^{?}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}c\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}f\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}^{?}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}b\ ,

which has smaller length.
Applying this procedure exhaustively yields a minimal
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path
a\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}b
.
∎

See
3.9

Proof.

We analyze each case separately.

Item

(i)
.

A minimal
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path between write events may only contain
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edges, and write-to-write and read-to-write
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edges.
By construction, every
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edge is non-decreasing.
Furthermore, by
Lemma

3.4
, every write-to-write and read-to-write
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge that does not contain bridge events is non-decreasing.
Therefore, we have
i\leq j
, as desired.

Item

(ii)
.

Similarly to the case above,
\mathcal{P}
may only contain non-decreasing edges.
Furthermore, since
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})\neq\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{j})
,
\mathcal{P}
must contain at least one
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edge, and, therefore, at least one read-to-write
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge.
This
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge is increasing due to
Item

(ii)
of
Lemma

3.4
.
Since all edges of
\mathcal{P}
are non-decreasing, and it has at least one increasing edge, we have
i<j
, as desired.
∎

See
3.8

Proof.

Let a bridge edge be an
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edge connecting bridge events.
Since every inner event in a minimal
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path is adjacent to an
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edge, any bridge event in
\mathcal{P}
must be an endpoint of a bridge edge in
\mathcal{P}
.
However, any bridge edge crosses between
\mathsf{Tid}_{x}
and
\mathsf{Tid}_{y}
, so returning to the originating thread domain requires at least a second bridge edge.
These two bridge edges would share a thread (from the topology in
Fig.

3
), introducing two
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edges on that thread into
\mathcal{P}
, contradicting with the fact that
\mathcal{P}
is minimal.
∎

See
3.10

Proof.

From
Lemma

3.7
, there exists a minimal
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path
\mathcal{P}\colon\mathtt{r}_{i}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
.
Since there are no outgoing
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-edges from
\{{t_{x}},t^{\prime}_{x}\}
or
\{{t_{y}},{t^{\prime}_{y}}\}
to any other thread,
\mathcal{P}
is fully contained in one of these two sets, and in particular contains no bridge events.
If
\mathcal{P}
is a single
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge, then
i<j
holds by definition.
Otherwise,
\mathcal{P}
has the form
\mathtt{r}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{\ell}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}^{?}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{\ell^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}^{?}$}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
;
we bound the indices in three steps.

•

i<\ell
: by
Item

(ii)
of
Lemma

3.4
, every
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge from a read to a non-bridge write is increasing.

•

\ell\leq\ell^{\prime}
: this sub-path only contains
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
and write-to-write
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edges.
The former are non-decreasing by construction;
the latter are non-decreasing by
Item

(i)
of
Lemma

3.4
.

•

\ell^{\prime}\leq j
: all decreasing read-to-read
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edges are listed in
Lemma

3.5
;
for all of them, the earlier event reads from a location written by a thread in
\{{t_{\alpha}^{x}},{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}},{t_{\alpha}^{y}},{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}\}
.
Since
\mathtt{r}_{\ell^{\prime}}
reads from
\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}}
, and
\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}}
is in one of
\{{t_{x}},t^{\prime}_{x}\}
or
\{{t_{y}},{t^{\prime}_{y}}\}
,
\mathtt{r}_{\ell^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}^{?}$}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
must be non-decreasing.

Combining these inequalities yields
i<\ell\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq j
, hence
i<j
.
∎

See
3.11

Proof.

We prove the case
\langle\mathtt{w}_{i}({{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}}),\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})\rangle
; the others are analogous.

Lemma

3.7
implies that there exists a minimal
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path
\mathcal{P}\colon\mathtt{w}_{i}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
.
Since
\mathtt{w}_{i},\mathtt{w}_{j}\in\mathsf{Tid}_{x}
,
Lemma

3.8
implies that
\mathcal{P}
contains no bridge events.
From
Fig.

3
, the only threads reachable from
\mathtt{w}_{i}
that can reach
\mathtt{w}_{j}
without crossing bridge edges (i.e.,
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edges connecting bridge events) are
\{{t_{\alpha}^{x}},{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}\}
, so
\mathcal{P}
is fully contained in
\{{t_{\alpha}^{x}},{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}\}
.
All internal events of
\mathcal{P}
are endpoints of
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edges connecting the threads
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
and
{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}
; all such edges are in locations
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}}
, so every internal event of
\mathcal{P}
has location in
\{{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{{z^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}}\}
.

Since
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})\neq\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{j})
,
\mathcal{P}
contains at least one
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edge, and so its final edge is a read-to-write
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge of the form
\mathtt{r}_{\ell}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
.
Let an iteration be an execution of the inner loop of
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
.
Since
\mathtt{r}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
is not executed in the first iteration of
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
, and it is executed exactly once in every other iteration, the event
\mathtt{r}_{\ell}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
is executed in the
(\ell+1)
-th iteration of
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
.
Since
\mathtt{w}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
is executed exactly once in each iteration of
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
, the event
\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
is executed in the
j
-th iteration of
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
.
Furthermore,
\mathtt{r}_{\ell}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})
is the last event the
(\ell+1)
-th iteration of
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
, so if
\mathtt{r}_{\ell}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}({t_{\alpha}^{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}})
, then it must be the case that
\ell+1<j
, or, equivalently
\ell<j-1
.
The remainder of
\mathcal{P}
contributes only non-decreasing steps (write-to-write and read-to-write
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edges by
Item

(i)
of
Lemma

3.4
, and
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edges by construction), so
i\leq\ell<j-1
.
∎

See
3.12

Proof.

Consider two write events
\mathtt{w}_{i}
,
\mathtt{w}_{j}
such that
\mathtt{w}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
and
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{i})=\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{j})=u
, and we argue that
i<j
.
If
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})=\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{j})
, the result follows from the irreflexivity of
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
: if
i>j
, then
\mathtt{w}_{j}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{w}_{i}
by construction, which, together with
\mathtt{w}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
, would violate
Lemma

3.6
.
Otherwise, we have that
u
must be written by more than one thread, which implies that
u\in\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}\}
.
Note that
u
is a non-bridge location, thus neither
\mathtt{w}_{i}
nor
\mathtt{w}_{j}
is a bridge event.
By
Lemma

3.7
, there exists a minimal
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path
\mathcal{P}\colon\mathtt{w}_{i}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{w}_{j}
.
Furthermore, the
x
-locations
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
are written exclusively by threads in
\mathsf{Tid}_{x}
, and the
y
-locations
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}}
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}
exclusively by threads in
\mathsf{Tid}_{y}
.
Therefore,
Lemma

3.8
implies that
\mathcal{P}
does not contain bridge events.
Then,
Lemma

3.9
applies to conclude that
\mathcal{P}
is increasing, thus
i<j
.
∎

See
3.13

Proof.

Consider two events
\mathtt{w}_{i},\mathtt{r}_{j}
such that
\mathtt{w}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
and
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{i})=\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{r}_{j})=u
, and we argue that
i\leq j
.
By
Lemma

3.7
, there exists a minimal
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path
\mathcal{P}:\mathtt{w}_{i}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
.
The proof proceeds by case analysis on the shape of
\mathcal{P}
, starting with simple cases and then handling the general form.

\mathcal{P}
is a single
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge.

This implies that
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})=\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{r}_{j})
, so
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})
both writes and reads on
u
.
The pair
\langle\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i}),u\rangle
can only be one of the following:

\begin{array}[]{llll}\langle{t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}}\rangle,&\langle{t_{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}\rangle,&\langle{t_{y}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}}\rangle,&\langle{t_{y}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}\rangle\\
\langle t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}}\rangle,&\langle t^{\prime}_{x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}\rangle,&\langle{t^{\prime}_{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}}\rangle,&\langle{t^{\prime}_{y}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}\rangle\\
\end{array}

In each case, writes and reads to
u
alternate in program order, so
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}^{\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})}\cap(\mathsf{W}_{u}\times\mathsf{R}_{u})
is non-decreasing, and thus
i\leq j
.

\mathcal{P}
ends in an
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edge.

Then
\mathcal{P}\colon\mathtt{w}_{i}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}^{?}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{w}_{j}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
.
Since
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{j})=u
,
Lemma

3.12
yields
i<j
.

\mathcal{P}
ends in a
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge.

Then
\mathcal{P}\colon\mathtt{w}_{i}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}^{?}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{w}_{\ell}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{\ell}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
.
This case is significantly more involved, and will be split further.
The sub-cases are determined by whether
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell})=u
and whether
\mathtt{w}_{i}
and
\mathtt{r}_{j}
are bridge events.

\mathtt{w}_{i}
and
\mathtt{r}_{j}
are bridge events.

Assume
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{i})=\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{r}_{j})={\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}}
, so
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})={t_{\alpha}^{x}}
and
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{r}_{j})={t_{\alpha}^{y}}
.
The other cases for
\langle\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{i}),\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i}),\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{r}_{j})\rangle
, namely,
\langle{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{\alpha}}},{t_{\alpha}^{y}},{t_{\alpha}^{x}}\rangle
,
\langle{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x,y}},{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}},{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}}\rangle
, and
\langle{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\ell_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}},{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}},{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}}\rangle
, are symmetric.
Every
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path from
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
to
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
must pass through an
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edge at location
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}}
(see
Fig.

3
), so
\mathcal{P}
has the form
\mathtt{w}_{i}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}^{?}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{\ell^{\prime}}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}^{?}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
with
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}})=\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{r}_{\ell^{\prime}})={\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}}
.
This implies that
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}})={t_{\alpha}^{x}}
and
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{r}_{\ell^{\prime}})={t_{\alpha}^{y}}
, since
{t_{\alpha}^{x}}
and
{t_{\alpha}^{y}}
are the only threads that write/read in
{\color[rgb]{0.76,0.01,0}z_{\alpha}^{x,y}}
, respectively.
Since
\mathcal{P}
is minimal and
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})=\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}})
, the sub-path
\mathtt{w}_{i}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}^{?}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}}
must be either empty or a
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge, giving
i\leq\ell^{\prime}
.
For the same reason,
\mathtt{r}_{\ell^{\prime}}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}^{?}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
must also be either empty or a
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edge, giving
\ell^{\prime}\leq j
;
combining these inequalities yields
i\leq j
, as desired.

\mathtt{w}_{i}
and
\mathtt{r}_{j}
are not bridge events,
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell})=u
.

If
\mathtt{w}_{i}=\mathtt{w}_{\ell}
, then clearly
i=\ell
.
Otherwise, we have
\mathtt{w}_{i}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}\mathtt{w}_{\ell}
, and
Lemma

3.12
implies that
i<\ell
.
Thus, in either case, we have
i\leq\ell
.
Moreover, by definition (
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-ordered same-location reads have increasing indices), we have
\ell<j
.
We thus arrive at
i<j
.

\mathtt{w}_{i}
and
\mathtt{r}_{j}
are not bridge events,
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell})\neq u
.

Since only bridge locations are accessed by threads in both
\mathsf{Tid}_{x}
and
\mathsf{Tid}_{y}
, and neither
\mathtt{w}_{i}
nor
\mathtt{r}_{j}
is a bridge event, we have that either both events belong to threads in
\mathsf{Tid}_{x}
, or both belong to threads in
\mathsf{Tid}_{y}
.
Then
Lemma

3.8
implies that
\mathcal{P}
does not contain bridge events.
Since
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{r}_{j})=\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{r}_{\ell})
and
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{r}_{j})\neq\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{r}_{\ell})
, we have that
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{r}_{j})
reads at more than one non-bridge location.
The only threads for which this is the case are
\{{t_{x}},{t_{y}},t^{\prime}_{x},{t^{\prime}_{y}}\}
, which allows us to assert that
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{r}_{j})
is a verifier thread.
We now consider two cases for
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})
: whether it is a verifier or a guesser thread.

•

\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})
is a verifier thread.

Since
\mathcal{P}
contains no bridge events,
Lemma

3.9
gives
i\leq\ell
.
From
Fig.

3
, verifier threads only have
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-paths to verifier threads, so
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell})\in\{{t_{x}},{t_{y}},t^{\prime}_{x},{t^{\prime}_{y}}\}
.
The final edge
\mathtt{r}_{\ell}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}$}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
is non-decreasing according to
Lemma

3.5
, since
\mathtt{r}_{\ell}
reads from a verifier thread.
Therefore
\ell\leq j
, and we arrive at
i\leq j
.

•

\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})
is a guesser thread.

We analyze the case of
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})\in\{{t_{\alpha}^{x}},{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}\}
, which implies that
c\in\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}}\}
.
The only reads in these locations are in
\{{t_{x}},t^{\prime}_{x}\}
, therefore
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{r}_{j})\in\{{t_{x}},t^{\prime}_{x}\}
.
The other cases for the tuple
\langle\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i}),\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{r}_{j}),c\rangle
, namely, when it is one of
\langle\{{t_{\alpha}^{y}},{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{y}}\},\{{t_{y}},{t^{\prime}_{y}}\},\{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{\alpha}}\}\rangle
,
\langle\{{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{x}},{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{x}}\},\{{t_{x}},t^{\prime}_{x}\},\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}\}\rangle
, and
\langle\{{t_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}^{y}},{{t^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}^{y}}\},\{{t_{y}},{t^{\prime}_{y}}\},\{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0.87}y_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}y^{\prime}_{{\mathchoice{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}{\vbox to4.30554pt{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\tenrm\beta$}\hss}}\hss}}\vss\par}}}}}\}\rangle
, are symmetric.
Any
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}
-path from some thread in
\{{t_{\alpha}^{x}},{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}\}
to some thread in
\{{t_{x}},t^{\prime}_{x}\}
must pass through an
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edge connecting a guesser thread to a verifier thread.
Specifically, it must contain an edge
\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{\ell^{\prime}}
with
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}})\in\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}}\}
(see
Figs.

3
and
5
);
therefore,
\mathcal{P}
has the form
\mathtt{w}_{i}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}^{?}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}$}}\mathtt{r}_{\ell^{\prime}}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}^{?}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
.

We consider the cases when
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{i})=\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}})
and when
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{i})\neq\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}})
.

•

\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{i})=\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}})
.

If
\mathtt{w}_{i}=\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}}
then
i=\ell^{\prime}
;
otherwise, we have that
\mathtt{w}_{i}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}}
and
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{i})=\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}})
, so
Lemma

3.12
gives
i<\ell^{\prime}
.
In either case,
i\leq\ell^{\prime}
.
Similarly, if
\mathtt{r}_{\ell^{\prime}}=\mathtt{r}_{j}
then
\ell^{\prime}=j
; otherwise
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{r}_{\ell^{\prime}})=\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{r}_{j})
and
Lemma

3.10
gives
\ell^{\prime}<j
.
In either case,
\ell^{\prime}\leq j
.
Combining the two inequalities yields
i\leq j
.

•

\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{i})\neq\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}})
.

Recall that
\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{i}),\mathsf{loc}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}})\in\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}}\}
, and

\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})\in\{{t_{\alpha}^{x}},{{t^{\prime}_{\alpha}}^{x}}\}
, and
\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}}
is written by a guesser thread.
Since different guesser threads write each of
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x_{\alpha}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{\prime}_{\alpha}}\}
, it must be the case that
\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{i})\neq\mathsf{tid}(\mathtt{w}_{\ell^{\prime}})
, so
Item

(ii)
of
Lemma

3.9
gives
i<\ell^{\prime}
.
The path
\mathtt{r}_{\ell^{\prime}}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
has the form
\mathtt{r}_{\ell^{\prime}}\mathrel{\overset{\smash{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}^{?}$}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}\mathtt{r}_{m}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}^{?}$}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
, where the sub-path to
\mathtt{r}_{m}
consists only of non-decreasing edges (
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edges by construction, read-to-write
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}
-edges by
Lemma

3.4
, as
\mathcal{P}
does not contain bridge events), giving
\ell^{\prime}\leq m
and thus
i<m
.
By
Lemma

3.5
, the final edge
\mathtt{r}_{m}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}po}^{?}$}}\mathtt{r}_{j}
decreases the index by at most one, so
m\leq j+1
;
combined with
i<m
, this yields
i\leq j
.

∎

Appendix B
Proofs of
Section

4

See
4.1

Proof.

We let
\pi=\pi_{1}\cdot\pi_{2}\cdots\pi_{\ell}
, and let
\mathsf{Sums}_{\mathcal{T}}
denote the set of possible summaries
\langle Q_{\mathcal{T}},\phi_{\mathcal{T}},\mathsf{Erf}_{\mathcal{T}}\rangle
.
The number of possible values for
Q_{\mathcal{T}}
,
\phi_{\mathcal{T}}
, and
\mathsf{Erf}_{\mathcal{T}}
are
2^{|\mathsf{P}.\mathcal{Q}|}
,
(|\mathsf{Val}|+1)^{|\mathsf{Loc}|}
, and
2^{|\mathsf{Loc}|}
, respectively.
Since
\mathsf{Loc}=O(1)
and
\mathsf{Val}=O(1)
, we have
|\mathsf{Sums}_{\mathcal{T}}|=O(2^{|\mathsf{P}.\mathcal{Q}|})
.
We first show that the size of each run
\pi_{c}
, with
c\in[\ell]
, is bounded.
If
|\pi_{c}|\leq|\mathsf{Sums}_{\mathcal{T}}|
, we are done. Otherwise, we proceed as follows.

Defining impeding events.

Let
\mathsf{R}_{\pi_{c}}
be the set of events in
\pi_{c+1},\ldots,\pi_{\ell}
that read from some event in
\pi_{c}
via an
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}
-edge.
Define
\mathsf{W}_{\pi_{c}}
as the image of
[\mathsf{R}_{\pi_{c}}];\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}rf}^{-1}
, i.e., the writing events of
\pi_{c}
that are read by some event in
\mathsf{R}_{\pi_{c}}
.
We call
\mathsf{W}_{\pi_{c}}
the impeding events of
\pi_{c}
.
Note that
|\mathsf{W}_{\pi_{c}}|\leq|\mathsf{R}_{\pi_{c}}|
.

Partitioning
\pi_{c}
into segments.

Partition
\pi_{c}
into
m+1
sequences of events
S_{1},\ldots,S_{m},S_{m+1}
, where
|S_{\mathsf{idx}}|=|\mathsf{Sums}_{\mathcal{T}}|+1
for
\mathsf{idx}\in[m]
and
|S_{m+1}|\leq|\mathsf{Sums}_{\mathcal{T}}|
.
By the pigeonhole principle, each segment
S_{\mathsf{idx}}
with
\mathsf{idx}\in[m]
contains a pair of events
\langle e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}},e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}}\rangle
with
e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}}<_{\pi}e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}}
and
\mathsf{Sum}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}})=\mathsf{Sum}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}})
.
Since
\langle G,(\pi_{1},\ldots,\pi_{\ell})\rangle
is irreducible, the pair
\langle e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}},e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}}\rangle
is not collapsible, which means at least one of the following must hold:

(1)

There exists
e_{b}\in\mathsf{W}_{\pi_{c}}
such that
e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}}<_{\pi}e_{b}\leq_{\pi}e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}}
.

(2)

For some
x\in\mathsf{Loc}
and
e\in(G.E\setminus G.E^{\mathsf{tid}(e_{i})})
, we have
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i},x)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}e
but
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j},x)\not\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize\hskip 5.0pt$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.4,0}hb}$}}e
; this implies there exists
e_{b}\in\mathsf{W}_{\pi_{c}}
such that
[\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}},x)]<_{\pi}e_{b}\leq_{\pi}[\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}},x)]
.

In either case, we say
e_{b}

impedes

\langle e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}},e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}}\rangle
from being reduced.

Bounding the number of impeded pairs per impeding event.

We now determine how many pairs
\langle e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}},e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}}\rangle
can be impeded by a single event
e_{b}\in\mathsf{W}_{\pi_{c}}
.
As helpful notation, let
\mathsf{Ew}_{\mathcal{T}}(e,x)
denote the minimal write
\mathtt{w}(t,x)
with respect to
\leq_{\pi_{c}}
such that
e\leq_{\pi_{c}}\mathtt{w}(t,x)
, or
\bot
if no such write exists.

(1)

If
e_{b}
impedes
\langle e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}},e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}}\rangle
via
(1)
, then
e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}}<_{\pi}e_{b}\leq_{\pi}e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}}
.
There is at most one
\mathsf{idx}\in[m]
satisfying this condition.

(2)

If
e_{b}
impedes
\langle e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}},e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}}\rangle
via
(2)
, then for some
x\in\mathsf{Loc}
, we have
[\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}},x)]<_{\pi}e_{b}\leq_{\pi}[\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}},x)]
.
By definition of
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}
, we have
[\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}},x)]\leq_{\pi}e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}}<_{\pi}[\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}},x)]\leq_{\pi}e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}}
.
We consider two subcases:

•

If
e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}}<_{\pi}e_{b}
, then
e_{b}
may only impede the unique pair
\langle e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}},e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}}\rangle
for which
e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}}<_{\pi}e_{b}\leq_{\pi}e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}}
.

•

If
e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}}\geq_{\pi}e_{b}
, then
e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}}<_{\pi}\mathsf{Ew}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{b},x)\leq_{\pi}e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}}
. There is at most one
\mathsf{idx}
satisfying this condition.

Therefore, for each location
x\in\mathsf{Loc}
, event
e_{b}
impedes at most one pair via criterion
(2)
.
Across all locations,
e_{b}
impedes at most
|\mathsf{Loc}|
pairs via
(2)
.

Combining both cases, each
e_{b}\in\mathsf{W}_{\pi_{c}}
impedes at most
|\mathsf{Loc}|+1
pairs of the form
\langle e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}},e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}}\rangle
from being reduced.

Deriving the bound.

Since every pair
\langle e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}},e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}}\rangle
with
\mathsf{idx}\in[m]
must be impeded by some event in
\mathsf{W}_{\pi_{c}}
, we have:

m\leq|\mathsf{W}_{\pi_{c}}|\cdot(|\mathsf{Loc}|+1)

Since
|\pi_{c}|=m\cdot(|\mathsf{Sums}_{\mathcal{T}}|+1)+|S_{m+1}|\leq m\cdot(|\mathsf{Sums}_{\mathcal{T}}|+1)+|\mathsf{Sums}_{\mathcal{T}}|
, we obtain:

|\pi_{c}|\leq|\mathsf{W}_{\pi_{c}}|\cdot(|\mathsf{Loc}|+1)\cdot(|\mathsf{Sums}_{\mathcal{T}}|+1)+|\mathsf{Sums}_{\mathcal{T}}|

Since
|\mathsf{W}_{\pi_{c}}|\leq|\mathsf{R}_{\pi_{c}}|\leq|\pi_{c+1}|+\ldots+|\pi_{\ell}|
, we have:

|\pi_{c}|\leq(|\pi_{c+1}|+\ldots+|\pi_{\ell}|)\cdot(|\mathsf{Loc}|+1)\cdot(|\mathsf{Sums}_{\mathcal{T}}|+1)+|\mathsf{Sums}_{\mathcal{T}}|

Let
g(c)
denote the maximum possible size of
\pi_{c}
. Then:

g(c)\leq|\mathsf{Sums}_{\mathcal{T}}|+(|\mathsf{Sums}_{\mathcal{T}}|+1)\cdot(|\mathsf{Loc}|+1)\cdot\sum_{j=c+1}^{\ell}g(j)

Since
\mathsf{Tid}
,
\mathsf{Loc}
, and
\mathsf{Val}
are bounded and
|\mathsf{Sums}_{\mathcal{T}}|=O(2^{|\mathsf{P}.\mathcal{Q}|})
, there exists a constant
C
such that
g(\ell)\leq C\cdot 2^{|\mathsf{P}.\mathcal{Q}|}
and for all
c<\ell
:

g(c)\leq C\cdot 2^{|\mathsf{P}.\mathcal{Q}|}\cdot\sum_{j=c+1}^{\ell}g(j)

Using
g(\ell)=O(2^{|\mathsf{P}.\mathcal{Q}|})
(as the last context has no impeding events), solving this recurrence yields

\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}g(j)=O(2^{|\mathsf{P}.\mathcal{Q}|\cdot\ell})

We conclude that
|G.E|=|\pi_{1}|+\ldots+|\pi_{\ell}|=O(2^{|\mathsf{P}.\mathcal{Q}|\cdot\ell})
.
∎

See
4.3

Proof.

This proof follows the same steps as
4.1
. We detail here the key changes.

Defining impeding events.
We add another set of impeding events, say,
\mathsf{RMW}_{\pi_{c}}
, which corresponds to the set of all RMW events in
\pi_{c}
.

Partitioning
\pi_{c}
into segments.

In this part, we must also incorporate condition
(v)
, that states that
\mathtt{rmw}\in\pi_{c}
may block a pair
\langle e_{i},e_{j}\rangle
from being reduced if, for some location
x
, we have
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i},x)\neq\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j},x)
and
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i},x)=\mathtt{rmw}
.

To do so, also consider the following condition for a pair
\langle e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}},e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}}\rangle
to not be collapsible:

(3)

There exists
e_{b}\in\mathsf{RMW}_{\pi_{c}}
such that

\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i},x)=e_{b}
and
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i},x)\neq\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j},x)
.

We can argue that if
e_{b}
impedes
\langle e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}},e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}}\rangle
via condition
3
, then
e_{b}
cannot simultaneously satisfy the same impeding condition for
\langle e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx^{\prime}}},e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx^{\prime}}}\rangle
. The reason for this is that for any
\mathsf{idx}<\mathsf{idx^{\prime}}
,
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}},x)=e_{b}
and
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}},x)\neq\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}},x)
implies
\mathsf{Lw}_{\mathcal{T}}(e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx^{\prime}}},x)\neq e_{b}
. Intuitively, after
e_{b}
is not the latest writing event on
x
anymore, that property cannot be recovered.

Bounding the number of impeded pairs per impeding event.

Per the argument above, each
e_{b}\in\mathsf{RMW}_{\pi_{c}}
impedes one pair of the form
\langle e_{i}^{\mathsf{idx}},e_{j}^{\mathsf{idx}}\rangle
from being reduced.

Deriving the bound.

We now initially have
m\leq|\mathsf{W}_{\pi_{c}}|\cdot(|\mathsf{Loc}|+1)+|\mathsf{RMW}_{\pi_{c}}|
.
Following the same steps as in
4.1
, we arrive at

\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}g(j)=O(2^{(|\mathsf{P}.\mathcal{Q}|+|G.E\cap\mathsf{RMW}|)\cdot\ell})

Which leads to the conclusion that
|G.E|=|\pi_{1}|+\ldots+|\pi_{\ell}|=O(2^{(|\mathsf{P}.\mathcal{Q}|+|G.E\cap\mathsf{RMW}|)\cdot\ell})
.

∎
